Gauging the Categorical Connes’ χ ~ ​ ( M ) \tilde{\chi}(M)
Quan Chen

Abstract
. We prove that if a finite group G G acts outerly on a McDuff II 1 \rm II_{1} factor M M , then 𝖱𝖾𝗉 ⁡ ( G / K ​ L ) \mathsf{Rep}(G/KL) is a braided monoidal full subcategory of the categorical Connes’ χ ~ ​ ( M ⋊ G ) \tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G) defined in arXiv:2111.06378, where K K and L L are the centrally trivial and approximately inner parts in G G respectively. When L L is trivial, we give an explicit formula for the G / K G/K -gauging procedure on χ ~ ​ ( M ⋊ G ) \tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G) . This is the categorical generalization of Connes’ short exact sequence on χ ⁡ ( M ⋊ G ) \chi(M\rtimes G) . Using this machinery, for any finite group G G , we construct a McDuff II 1 \rm II_{1} factor M M , whose χ ~ ​ ( M ) \tilde{\chi}(M) is braided equivalent to 𝖱𝖾𝗉 ⁡ ( G ) \mathsf{Rep}(G) . This is the first example of a braided fusion category which is not modular as χ ~ \tilde{\chi} .



1.
Introduction

Tensor categories provide a fundamental algebraic language for describing symmetries in a wide range of mathematical and physical contexts, from topological quantum field theory to bimodules of operator algebras.
A pivotal example arises from Jones’ subfactor theory, where the standard invariant of a finite index
\rm II_{1}
-subfactor
[
Pop94b
,
Jon22
]
is captured by a unitary tensor category, together with a chosen Q-system in this category
[
Müg03
,
Lon89
,
GS12
,
JP19
,
CHPJP22
]
.

In Algebraic Quantum Field Theory (AQFT), the principles of locality and covariance are encoded in a local net of operator algebras, and the Doplicher-Haag-Roberts (DHR) program describes superselection sectors as a braided tensor category
[
DHR71
,
FRS89
,
Haa96
,
KLM01
,
Oga22
,
Jon24
]
. A parallel and powerful theme is the gauging of a finite global symmetry, which studies the transition of a theory enriched by symmetry defects into a new dynamical phase.
Taking the fixed-point net under an action of a finite group
G
yields a gauged theory, whose representation category is the
G
-equivariantization of the original theory’s category
[
Müg05
]
.
Similarly, in the context of topological phases, gauging a global symmetry group results in a new phase whose excitations are described by the
G
-equivariantization of the original category
[
Kit06
]
. These constructions highlight a universal mechanism: passing from a symmetric system to its orbifold quotient is categorically realized by equivariantization.

This paper situates these ideas within the realm of
\rm II_{1}
factors.
In the recent work, we introduce the categorical Connes’ invariant
\tilde{\chi}(M)

[
CJP24
]
, a unitary braided tensor category constructed from bimodules over a
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
that are both approximately inner and centrally trivial.
This invariant generalizes Connes’ original group-theoretic invariant
\chi(M)

[
Con75
]
and Jones’ quadratic form
\kappa

[
Jon80a
]
.
Our goal is to develop a gauging framework for this new invariant.

Theorem A
.

Let
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
be an outer action of a finite group
G
on a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.
Let
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
and
L=\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))\cap G
, then

\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
is a unitary braided full subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
, where
\operatorname{Ct}(M)
is the group of centrally trivial automorphisms, and
{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
is the group of approximately inner automorphisms.

The proof of this theorem relies on two key technical observations, which are of independent interest.
This shows how the representation theory factors through the two components of Connes’ invariant:
Let
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
be an outer action of a finite group
G
on a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.
From the natural embedding
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)\text{ is approximately inner;}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)\text{ is centrally trivial.}

As a full tensor subcategory,
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)\subseteq\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)\cap\mathsf{Rep}(G/L)\subseteq{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes G)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes G)=\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
is also a braided subcategory.

Gauging is a well-known procedure of group equivariantization and deequivariantization
[
Müg05
,
ENO05
,
DGNO10
]
.

\left\{\parbox{128.0374pt}{\rm\centering Braided tensor categories \\
containing $\mathsf{Rep}(G)$ \\
as braided subcategory\@add@centering}\right\}
\left\{\parbox{99.58464pt}{\centering$G$-crossed braided \\
tensor categories\@add@centering}\right\}
deequivariantization
G
-equivariantization

From the categorical point of view, a
G
-crossed braided tensor category is a 3-category with one object, and the set of 1-morphisms is equivalent to the group
G

[
JPR23
]
. The
G
-crossed braiding arises as the interchanger of 3-categories.
The deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
as a
G/KL
-crossed braided tensor category suggests a stronger evidence that von Neumann algebras should be objects in a yet to be discovered 3-category.

Theorem B
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
M
is a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor.
Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an outer action and
\psi
is not approximately inner.
Then the
G/K
-deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G/K}\cong\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)

as unitary
G/K
-crossed braided tensor categories, where
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
.

Jones discusses it in his notes on
\chi(M)

[
Jon
]
about the Connes’ short exact sequence:

Theorem
.

[
Jon
]

Suppose the outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner.
There exist group homomorphisms
\delta:\widehat{G/K}\to\chi(M\rtimes G)
and
\Pi:\chi(M\rtimes G)\to(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)
such that

0\to\widehat{G/K}\xrightarrow{\delta}\chi(M\rtimes G)\xrightarrow{\Pi}(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)\to 0

is a short exact sequence, where
\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
is
the group of
G
-approximately inner automorphisms, consisting of
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
such that there exists a sequence of unitaries
(u_{n})\subseteq M^{G}
with
\|\alpha(x)-u_{n}xu_{n}^{*}\|_{2}\to 0
for all
x\in M
;

\mathrm{GCt}(M)
is the group of
G
-centrally trivial automorphisms, consisting of
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
that there exists
g\in G
with
\|\alpha(a_{n})-\psi_{g}(a_{n})\|_{2}\to 0
for all central sequence
(a_{n})\in M_{\omega}=M^{\prime}\cap M^{\omega}
.

This short exact sequence is one of the motivations for applying the
gauging paradigm
to
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.
The group characters can be viewed as
1
-dimensional representations. Therefore, Theorem A is the categorical generalization of
\delta:\widehat{G/K}\to\chi(M\rtimes G)
.

In general, suppose
G
is a finite group,
{\mathcal{B}}
is a braided
\rm C^{*}
-tensor category and
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}
is a unitary braided fully faithful tensor subcategory.
We prove that

0\to\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}})\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}\to 0

is a short exact sequence, where

\operatorname{Inv}(-)
is the group of isomorphism classes of invertible objects and

\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}=\{[y]\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})\mid y\ \mathrm{admits\ a\ unitary\ }G\mathrm{-equivariant\ structure}\}

We show that the invertible liftable part of the
G/K
-deequivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
is equivalent to
(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)
.
This entails that the gauging procedure is a complete categorical generalization of Connes’ short exact sequence.

Theorem C
.

Suppose the outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner and
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
.
The group of invertible
G/K
-liftable bimodules in the
G/K
-deequivariantization
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G/K}
is equivalent to the group of the intersection of
G
-centrally trivial automorphisms and
G
-approximately inner automorphisms
\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
modulo inner automorphisms, i.e.,

\operatorname{Inv}\left(\bigoplus\limits_{g\in G/K}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)\right)_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M).

In
[
Pop10
]
, Popa studies Connes’
\chi(M)
of a countable infinite spatial tensor product of non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factors
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N_{i}
.
He shows
\chi(M)
is trivial, and
M
is McDuff but not s-McDuff (
M
is s-McDuff if
M
is isomorphic to
R\overline{\otimes}N
for some non-Gamma
N
), which gives a negative answer to Connes’ question
[
Con75
]
.
Building off techniques of Popa and
[
CJP24
]
, we prove that the categorical Connes’
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
is trivial.
This also gives a negative answer to
[
CJP24
]
’s generalized version of Connes’ question:
whether it is true that for a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
,
M
is s-McDuff if and only if
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
.

Theorem D
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
N
is a non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor and
\alpha:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(N)
is an outer action. Let
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N
be the countable infinite spatial tensor product of
N
and
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is given by
\psi_{g}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{g}
.
Then
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes_{\psi}G)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as unitary braided tensor categories.

To prove this result, we have to use the gauging construction established before.
We first show that the group action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner nor centrally trivial, then by Theorem B,
the
G
-deequivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
,

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G}\cong\bigoplus_{g\in G}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)\boxtimes_{M}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M).

The
e
-grading component is
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
.
We also prove that
{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)\boxtimes_{M}X
cannot be approximately inner, when
X
is centrally trivial.
In other words, the
g
-grading component is empty when
g\neq e
.
Therefore,

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G}\cong\mathsf{Hilb}.

By the equivariantization/deequivariantization correspondence,

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)\cong(\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G})^{G}\cong\mathsf{Hilb}^{G}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G).

Corollary E
.

For any finite group
G
, its unitary representation category
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
can be realized as
\tilde{\chi}(M)
for some McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.

Since
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
is a symmetric braided tensor category, it is not modular.
Thus, we obtain an example of a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor whose
\tilde{\chi}(M)
is not modular.

Corollary F
.

There exist McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factors
M
whose
\tilde{\chi}(M)
is not modular.

Outline.

In §
2
, we recall the categorical Connes’
\tilde{\chi}(M)
and Connes’ short exact sequence.

In §
3
, we first prove
\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)\subseteq\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is approximately inner and
\mathsf{Rep}(G/L)\subseteq\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is centrally trivial.
Then we prove that
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)\subseteq\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
is braided.

In §
4
, we unpack the gauging procedure, which is necessary for computing the deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.
We prove that the general gauging construction induces a short exact sequence of Connes’ type.
Then we prove that the gauging construction indeed generalizes Connes’ short exact sequence.

In §
5
, for
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N_{i}
with non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor
N_{i}
, we show that
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
is trivial.
For any finite group
G
, we consider the entry-wise action on
M
for
N_{i}=N
.
We show that it is neither approximately inner nor centrally trivial. Then by the gauging construction, we obtain that
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.

\chi(M)
to my attention.

by the Department of Mathematics at Vanderbilt University.

2.
Background

2.1.
The central sequence algebra

Let
M
be a von Neumann algebra and
\omega
a free ultrafilter on
{\mathbb{N}}
.
We denote by
M^{\omega}=\{(x_{n})\mid x_{n}\in M,\ \sup\|x_{n}\|<\infty\}/\{(x_{n})\mid\lim_{n\to\omega}\|x_{n}\|_{2}=0\}
the ultrapower algebra, and we let
M_{\omega}:=M^{\prime}\cap M^{\omega}
be the central sequence algebra.

We say
M
is a
McDuff

\rm II_{1}
factor if
M\cong M\overline{\otimes}R
, where
R
is the hyperfinite
\rm II_{1}
factor.
McDuff shows that
M_{\omega}
is either abelian or a
\rm II_{1}
von Neumann algebra, and it is abelian if and only if
M
is not McDuff
[
McD70
]
.
Furthermore, Connes profoundly utilizes central sequences in his groundbreaking proof of injectivity implies hyperfiniteness for
\rm II_{1}
factors
[
Con76
]
.

Every automorphism
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
determines an automorphism
\alpha_{\omega}
of
M_{\omega}
via
\alpha_{\omega}((x_{n}))=(\alpha(x_{n}))
.

2.2.
Connes’ original
\chi(M)

For a given
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
and a free ultrafilter
\omega
on
{\mathbb{N}}
, we call an automorphism
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
approximately inner if there exists a sequence of unitaries
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\omega}
such that

\lim_{n\to\omega}\|\alpha(x)-u_{n}xu_{n}^{*}\|_{2}=0

for all
x\in M
.
It is clear that the composition of approximately inner automorphisms remains approximately inner.
We denote the group of approximately inner automorphisms by
\overline{\operatorname{Int}}(M)
.

We call an automorphism
\beta\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
centrally trivial if for any central sequence
(x_{n})_{n\in\omega}\in M_{\omega}
,

\lim_{n\to\omega}\|\beta(x_{n})-x_{n}\|_{2}=0.

In other words,
\beta
is centrally trivial if and only if the induced automorphism
\beta_{\omega}
on
M_{\omega}
is the identity.
Similarly, it is clear that the composition of centrally trivial automorphisms remains centrally trivial.
We denote the group of centrally trivial automorphisms by
\operatorname{Ct}(M)
.

In
[
Con75
]
, Connes introduced an invariant
\chi(M)
of a
\rm II_{1}
-factor
M
by

\chi(M):=(\operatorname{Ct}(M)\cap{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M),

a subgroup of
\operatorname{Out}(M)
consisting of the image of approximately inner and centrally trivial automorphisms.
Connes showed that
\alpha\beta\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\in\operatorname{Int}(M)
, for any
\alpha\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
,
\beta\in\operatorname{Ct}(M)
, which implies
\chi(M)
is an abelian group.
In
[
Jon80a
]
, Jones defined the
\kappa
invariant, which is a quadratic form on
\chi(M)
.
By using
(\chi(M),\kappa)
, Jones constructed a
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
which is antiisomorphic to itself but has no involutory antiautomorphic.
Therefore, these
\rm II_{1}
factors are not group von Neumann algebras.

From the tensor category perspective, Eilenberg and MacLane showed that an abelian group together with a quadratic form defines a braided 2-group
[
EM54
]
,
which gives a braided tensor category under linearization. This indicates the existence of a bimodule version of
\chi
as a braided tensor category generalizing both Connes’
\chi(M)
and Jones’
\kappa
invariant.

2.3.
Categorical Connes’
\tilde{\chi}(M)

In this section, we recall the construction of categorical Connes’
\chi(M)
, including approximately inner and centrally trivial bimodules, and properties from

[
CJP24
]
.

Let
M
be a
\rm II_{1}
factor with faithful normal trace
\operatorname{tr}_{M}
.
We denote by
\mathsf{Bim}(M)
the category of right
\rm W^{*}
-correspondences as in
[
CHPJP22
, Def. 2.15]
and by
\mathsf{Bim_{fgp}}(M)
a subcategory with finite generated projective bimodules.
In this paper, we only consider finite generated projective (or bifinite) bimodules, and we will denote them as
\mathsf{Bim}(M)
below.

For
X\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)
, we define the norm of
x\in X
as

\|x\|_{2}^{2}:=\|\langle x|x\rangle_{M}^{X}\|_{2}

where
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle_{M}^{X}
is the
M
-valued inner product on
X
and
\|\cdot\|_{2}
the 2-norm on
M
defined by
\|a\|_{2}:=\operatorname{tr}(a^{*}a)^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
a\in M
.

Definition 2.1
.

For
X\in\mathsf{Mod_{fgp}}(M)
, an
approximate
X_{M}
-basis
is a sequence
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}_{i=1}^{K}\subseteq X
,

\sup\limits_{i,n}\|x_{i}^{(n)}\|<\infty
,
and

\lim_{n\to\omega}\left\|x-\sum_{i=1}^{K}x_{i}^{(n)}\langle x_{i}^{(n)}|x\rangle_{M}^{X}\right\|_{2}=0\qquad\qquad\forall\,x\in X.

For
X\in\mathsf{Bim}(X)
, an
approximately inner

X_{M}
-basis is an approximate
X_{M}
-basis such that

\lim_{n\to\omega}\left\|ax_{i}^{(n)}-x_{i}^{(n)}a\right\|_{2}=0\qquad\qquad\forall\,a\in M.

(1)

We call a bimodule
X

approximately inner
over
M
if and only if there exists an approximately inner
X_{M}
-basis.

Definition 2.2
.

We call a bimodule
Y\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)

centrally trivial
if for all central sequences
(a_{n})\in M_{\omega}
and for all
y\in Y
,

\lim_{n\to\omega}\|a_{n}y-ya_{n}\|_{2}=0.

Note that all the approximately inner bimodules form a tensor subcategory of
\mathsf{Bim}(M)
, called
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M)
and all the centrally trivial bimodules form a tensor subcategory called
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
.

There is a centralizing structure
u
between
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M)
and
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
:
For
X,Y\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)
,
choosing approximately inner
X_{M}
-bases
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}_{i}

and ordinary
Y_{M}
-bases
\{y_{j}\}_{j}
respectively,
the unitary braiding
u_{X,Y}:X\boxtimes_{M}Y\xrightarrow{\sim}Y\boxtimes_{M}X
can be expressed as the following
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-limits of
M
-finite rank operators:

u_{X,Y}=\lim_{n\to\omega}\sum_{i,j}|y_{j}\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\rangle\langle x_{i}^{(n)}\boxtimes y_{j}|.

Note that
u_{X,Y}
is independent of the choice of approximately inner
X_{M}
-basis and
Y_{M}
-basis.
When we take
\tilde{\chi}(M):={\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)\subseteq\mathsf{Bim}(M)
be the full subcategory of bimodules that are both approximately inner and centrally trivial,
\tilde{\chi}(M)
is a unitary braided tensor category.

There are several properties and results for
\tilde{\chi}(M)
proved in
[
CJP24
]
:

Facts 2.3
.

(1)

Let
N\subseteq M
be a finite index
\rm II_{1}
subfactor such that
Q:={}_{N}L^{2}(M)_{N}
is a commutative Q-system in
\tilde{\chi}(N)
. Then
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\tilde{\chi}(N)^{\operatorname{loc}}_{Q}
as braided unitary tensor categories.

(2)

Suppose
N\subseteq M
is a finite depth non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
subfactor and
M_{\infty}
is the inductive limit of the basic construction. Then
\tilde{\chi}(M_{\infty})
is equivalent to the Drinfeld center of the standard invariant
{\mathcal{C}}(N\subseteq M)
as unitary braided tensor categories.

(3)

Suppose
\rm II_{1}
factors
M
and
N
are Morita equivalent (or called stably isomorphic for
\rm II_{1}
factors), then
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\tilde{\chi}(N)
as unitary braided tensor categories.

(4)

\tilde{\chi}(R)\cong\tilde{\chi}(N)\cong\tilde{\chi}(R\overline{\otimes}N)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
, where
R
is the hyperfinite
\rm II_{1}
factor and
N
is non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor.

2.4.
Connes’ short exact sequence

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
M
is a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
-factor,
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an outer action.
Suppose
\psi
is not approximately inner and
K=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
.
We say
\alpha
is
G
-approximately inner
, denoted
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
, if there exists a sequence of unitaries
(u_{n})\subseteq M^{G}
such that
\|\alpha(x)-u_{n}xu_{n}^{*}\|_{2}\to 0
for all
x\in M
.
We say
\alpha
is
G
-centrally trivial
, denoted
\alpha\in\mathrm{GCt}(M)
, if there exists
g\in G
, such that
\|\alpha(a_{n})-\psi_{g}(a_{n})\|_{2}\to 0
for all central sequences
(a_{n})\in M_{\omega}
.
In other words, the induced automorphism
\alpha_{\omega}=(\psi_{g})_{\omega}
on
M_{\omega}
for some
g\in G
.

Theorem 2.5
.

[
Jon
]

There exist group homomorphisms
\delta:\widehat{G/K}\to\chi(M\rtimes G)
and
\Pi:\chi(M\rtimes G)\to(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)
such that

0\to\widehat{G/K}\xrightarrow{\delta}\chi(M\rtimes G)\xrightarrow{\Pi}(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)\to 0

is a short exact sequence.

This short exact sequence provides a tool for calculating
\chi(M\rtimes G)
from the data of
M
and the action
\psi
on
M
.

3.
Representation subcategories in
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)

Inspired by the orbifold construction on
\chi(M)
for Connes’ short exact sequence
[
Jon
]
(see also
[
EK98
, §15]
), orbifold algebraic quantum field theory
[
Müg05
]
and the observation in
[
CJP24
, Thm. B]
, we give a categorical gauging construction on
\tilde{\chi}(M)
in this section.

Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an outer action of a finite group
G
on a
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.
Recall that in the crossed product
M\rtimes_{\psi}G
, every element can be uniquely expressed as
\sum\limits_{g\in G}a_{g}u_{g}
,
a_{g}\in M
, where

\displaystyle u_{g}u_{h}

\displaystyle=u_{gh}\text{ and }u_{g}^{*}=u_{g^{-1}}

\displaystyle u_{g}x

\displaystyle=\psi_{g}(x)u_{g}\text{ for }x\in M

Then
(xu_{g})^{*}=u_{g}^{*}x^{*}=u_{g^{-1}}x^{*}=\psi_{g^{-1}}(x^{*})u_{g^{-1}}
.
It is known that when
\psi
is outer,
M\rtimes G
is also a
\rm II_{1}
factor.

Let
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
be the category of unitary
G
-representations, which is a symmetric braided tensor category.
There is a canonical embedding
F:\mathsf{Rep}(G)\to\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
defined by
(H,\pi)\mapsto{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
.
Here the left and right
M\rtimes G
-module structures are given by

b_{h}u_{h}\rhd(\xi\otimes a_{g}u_{g}):=\pi_{h}(\xi)\otimes(b_{h}u_{h}a_{g}u_{g})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad(\xi\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\lhd b_{h}u_{h}:=\xi\otimes(a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h})

and the
M\rtimes G
-valued inner product is given by

\langle\xi\otimes a_{g}u_{g}\mid\eta\otimes b_{h}u_{h}\rangle:=\langle\xi\mid\eta\rangle(a_{g}u_{g})^{*}(b_{h}u_{h}).

For
(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G)
,
\eta\in H
,
\xi\in K
, we define the tensorator
F^{2}_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)}
for
F
:

\displaystyle F^{2}_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)}:

\displaystyle F(H,\pi)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes G}F(K,\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}F({}_{\pi\otimes\psi}(H\otimes K))

\displaystyle(\eta\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\boxtimes(\xi\otimes b_{h}u_{h})=(\eta\otimes 1)\boxtimes(\sigma_{g}(\xi)\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h})\mapsto(\eta\otimes\sigma_{g}(\xi))\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

Note that there is a natural symmetric braiding
\beta
on
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
One can check that

F(\beta_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)})((\eta\otimes\xi)\otimes a_{g}u_{g}))=(\xi\otimes\eta)\otimes a_{g}u_{g}.

It is clear that
\{\eta_{i}\otimes u_{1}\}_{i}
is a Pimsner-Popa
({}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G)_{M\rtimes_{\psi}G}
-basis, where
\{\eta_{i}\}
is an ONB for
H
.

Suppose
N
is a normal subgroup, then
\mathsf{Rep}(G/N)
is a braided tensor subcategory of
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
Indeed, we can construct a braided tensor functor
F:\mathsf{Rep}(G/N)\to\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as follows:
for an object
(H,\pi)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/N)
, we define

F(H,\pi):=(H,\pi^{\prime}),

where
\pi^{\prime}:G\to B(H)
is given by
\pi^{\prime}(g)=\pi(gN)
.

For a morphism
f\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/N)
, we define

F(f):=f

Here, for
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
, the group of approximately inner automorphisms
{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
and the group of centrally trivial automorphisms
\operatorname{Ct}(M)
are normal subgroups of
\operatorname{Out}(M)
.
Therefore, for outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
,
G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
and
G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))
are normal subgroups of
G
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(G)\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G),

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Rep}(G)\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)

3.1.
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))
is approximately inner

In this section, we show that
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is approximately inner.
By unpacking the embedding
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
, every representation
(H,\pi)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))
can be viewed as a
G
-representation, in particular
\pi_{g}=\operatorname{id}_{H}
if
\psi_{g}\in\operatorname{Ct}(M)
.

We first recall the definition and properties of a cocycle/coboundary of a group action.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
M
be a
\rm II_{1}
factor and
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an action.
We call
w:G\to U(M)
a
cocycle
(or 1-cocycle) if
w_{g}\psi_{g}(w_{h})=w_{gh}
for all
g,h\in G
.
Equivalently,
\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g}w_{g}u_{g}
is a projection in
M\rtimes G
.

A cocycle
w
is called a
coboundary
if there exists a unitary
t\in N
such that
w_{g}=t^{*}\psi_{g}(t)
for all
g\in G
.

Example 3.2
.

Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an action
and
(H,\pi)
is a finite dimensional
G
-representation.
Then
\pi:G\to B(H)=B(H)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow B(H)\otimes M
is automatically a cocycle for the
G
-action
\tilde{\psi}:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(B(H)\otimes M)
by
\tilde{\psi}_{g}(a\otimes m)=a\otimes\psi_{g}(m)
for
g\in G
,
a\in B(H)
, and
m\in M
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\pi(g)\tilde{\psi}_{g}(\pi(h))=(\pi_{g}\otimes 1)\tilde{\psi}_{g}(\pi_{h}\otimes 1)=(\pi_{g}\otimes 1)(\pi_{h}\otimes\psi_{g}(1))=\pi_{g}\pi_{h}\otimes 1=\pi_{gh}\otimes 1=\pi(gh).

Let
N=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M))
, which is a normal subgroup of
G
.
For each
h\in N
, since
\psi_{h}
is inner, there exists
v_{h}\in U(M)
such that
\psi_{h}=\operatorname{Ad}(v_{h})
.

Definition 3.3
.

[
Jon80b
, §1.2]

Given a group action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
, a
section
is a choice of unitaries
\{v_{h}\}_{h\in N}
such that

v_{h}v_{k}=\mu(h,k)v_{hk}\qquad\text{ for }h,k\in N,

where
\mu:N\times N\to U(1)
is a 2-cocycle; and

\psi_{g}(v_{g^{-1}hg})=\lambda(g,h)v_{h}\qquad\text{ for }g\in G,\ h\in N,

where
\lambda:G\times N\to U(1)
is compatible with
\psi
and
\mu
.

It seems
\mu
and
\lambda
are dependent to the choice of
\{v_{h}\}_{h\in N}
. In fact,
Jones proves that the cohomology class
[\mu,\lambda]
, which he calls the characteristic invariant, is an invariant of the group action
\psi

[
Jon80b
, Def. 2.8]
.
It is essential for the classification of finite group actions on the hyperfinite
\rm II_{1}
factor up to conjugacy
[
Jon80b
, Thm. 1.4.8]
.

For our purpose, the notion of section provides a characterization whether a cocycle is a coboundary.

Fact 3.4
.

[
Jon80b
, §3.1]

Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an action and
w
is a cocycle.
The following are equivalent:

(1)

w
is a coboundary;

(2)

the projections
\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g}w_{g}u_{g}
and
\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g}u_{g}
are von Neumann equivalent in
M\rtimes G
;

(3)

\operatorname{tr}(w_{h}v_{h})=\operatorname{tr}(v_{h})
for all
h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M))
, where
\{v_{h}\}
is a section of the action
\psi
.

Remark 3.5
.

When
\psi
is outer, then any 1-cocycle is a coboundary.
This is known as Ocneanu’s cocycle vanishing theorem.

Lemma 3.6
.

[
Con76
]

For the embedding
\Phi:\operatorname{Aut}(M)\to\operatorname{Aut}(M_{\omega})
given by
\alpha\mapsto\alpha_{\omega}
,
we have
\operatorname{Ct}(M)=\Phi^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M_{\omega}))
.

Proof.

If
\alpha\in\operatorname{Ct}(M)
, then
\alpha_{\omega}
is the identity on
M_{\omega}
, which is inner. Thus,
\operatorname{Ct}(M)\subseteq\Phi^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M_{\omega}))
.

Suppose there is an
\alpha
that is not centrally trivial
but the induced action
\alpha_{\omega}
is inner, then
\alpha_{\omega}=\operatorname{Ad}V
for some unitary
V=(v_{n})\in M_{\omega}
.
Note that if
\alpha
is not centrally trivial, then there exists
X=(x_{n})\in M_{\omega}
such that
\alpha_{\omega}(X)\neq X
in
M_{\omega}
.
Moreover, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that we can pick a subsequence (still denoted as
X=(x_{n})
) with
\|\alpha(x_{n})-x_{n}\|_{2}>\epsilon
for every
n
.
For each fixed
n
, since
X
is a central sequence, there exists
(k_{n})
such that
\|[v_{n},x_{k_{n}}]\|_{2}=\|v_{n}x_{k_{n}}v_{n}^{*}-x_{k_{n}}\|_{2}<\frac{1}{n}
.
This implies that for
Y=(x_{k_{n}})\in M_{\omega}
,
\alpha_{\omega}(Y)-Y=VYV^{*}-Y=0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

For
\tilde{\psi}:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(B(H)\otimes M)
given by
\tilde{\psi}_{g}:=\operatorname{id}_{B(H)}\otimes\psi_{g}
,
we have

G\cap\tilde{\psi}^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(B(H)\otimes M))=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M)).

Proof.

It is clear that
\supseteq
holds, since if
\psi_{g}
is inner, then
\tilde{\psi}_{g}=\operatorname{id}\otimes\psi_{g}
is inner.
We shall prove the other inclusion.
Suppose
\tilde{\psi}_{g}
is inner, then there exists
U\in B(H)\otimes M
such that
\tilde{\psi}_{g}=\operatorname{Ad}(U)
.
In particular,

\tilde{\psi}_{g}(1\otimes m)=U(1\otimes m)U^{*}=1\otimes\psi_{g}(m).

We can rewrite this as

U(1\otimes m)=(1\otimes\psi_{g}(m))U.

Since
H
is finite-dimensional, we can write
U=(U_{ij})_{i,j}
where
U_{ij}\in M
via the identification
B(H)\otimes M\cong M_{n}(M)
.
Additionally,
1\otimes m=mI_{n}
and
1\otimes\psi_{g}(m)=\psi_{g}(m)I_{n}
, where
I_{n}\in B(H)
is the identity matrix, then the above equation gives

(U_{ij})(mI_{n})=(\psi_{g}(m)I_{n})(U_{ij}).

This implies

U_{ij}m=\psi_{g}(m)U_{ij}

holds for all
i,h
and
m\in M
. Since
U
is a unitary, there exists a
U_{ij}\neq 0
. Choose one such nonzero
U_{ij}=:x
, then
xm=\psi_{g}(m)x
for all
m\in M
, we shall show
\psi_{g}
is inner.

First,

\displaystyle x^{*}xm

\displaystyle=x^{*}(xm)=x^{*}(\psi_{g}(m)x)=(x^{*}\psi_{g}(m))x=(\psi_{g}(m)^{*}x)^{*}x

\displaystyle=(\psi_{g}(m^{*})x)^{*}x=(xm^{*})^{*}x=mx^{*}x

holds for all
m\in M

this implies
x^{*}x\in Z(M)\cong{\mathbb{C}}1
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\psi_{g}(m)xx^{*}

\displaystyle=(\psi_{g}(m)x)x^{*}=xmx^{*}=x(xm^{*})^{*}

\displaystyle=x(\psi_{g}(m^{*})x)^{*}=xx^{*}\psi_{g}(m^{*})^{*}=xx^{*}\psi_{g}(m)

holds for all
m\in M
,
this implies
xx^{*}\in Z(M)\cong{\mathbb{C}}1
.
Combining these two results, let
\mu=\operatorname{tr}(x^{*}x)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\operatorname{tr}(xx^{*})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have
u:=\frac{x}{\mu}
is a unitary.
Then

um=\mu xm=\mu\psi_{g}(m)x=\psi_{g}(m)u,

which implies
\psi_{g}
is inner.
∎

Notation 3.8
.

We use the same convention as in
[
CJP24
, Nota. 4.3]
. For norm bounded nets
(f_{n})_{n},(g_{n})_{n}\subseteq\operatorname{Hom}(X_{M}\to Y_{M})
, and
f,g\in\operatorname{Hom}(X_{M}\to Y_{M})
, we write

•

f_{n}\approx g_{n}
if
\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\|f_{n}-g_{n}\|_{2}=0
;

•

f_{n}\approx f
if
\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\|f_{n}-f\|_{2}=0
.

Therefore,
f\approx g
if and only if
f=g
.

Theorem 3.9
.

\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is approximately inner.

Proof.

We denote
K=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
.
The outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
gives an induced action
\psi_{\omega}=\Phi\circ\psi
on the central sequence algebra
\psi_{\omega}:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M_{\omega})
.
For a given
(H,\pi)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)\subseteq\mathsf{Rep}(G)
, by Example
3.2
,
\pi:G\to B(H)\hookrightarrow B(H)\otimes M_{\omega}
is a cocycle for
\tilde{\psi}_{\omega}
.

By Lemma
3.6
, if the induced action
\psi_{h}
is inner, then
\psi_{h}
is centrally trivial.
Note that for any section
\{v_{h}\}
of
\tilde{\psi}_{\omega}
, by Lemma
3.7
, since

h\in G\cap\tilde{\psi}_{\omega}^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(B(H)\otimes M_{\omega}))=G\cap\psi_{\omega}^{-1}(\operatorname{Int}(M_{\omega}))=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M)),

we have
\pi_{h}=\operatorname{id}_{H}
so that
\operatorname{tr}(\pi_{h}v_{h})=\operatorname{tr}(v_{h})
.
According to Fact
3.4
, the cocycle
\pi
is a coboundary, i.e., there exists a unitary
t\in B(H)\otimes M_{\omega}
such that
\pi_{g}=t^{*}(\tilde{\psi}_{\omega})_{g}(t)
for all
g\in G
.

Let
\{\eta_{i}\}
be an ONB for
H
and
\{\varepsilon_{ij}\}
be the corresponding system of matrix units for
B(H)
.
We write
t=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}\otimes t_{ij}
for
t_{ij}=(t_{ij;n})_{n\in\omega}\in M_{\omega}
. Then we have

\displaystyle t^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}^{*}\otimes t_{ij}^{*}=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}\otimes t_{ji}^{*},

\displaystyle tt^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{ik}t_{jk}^{*},

\displaystyle t^{*}t

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{ki}^{*}t_{kj}.

Note that
t\in U(B(H)\otimes M_{\omega})
, we have

\sum_{k}t_{ik}t_{jk}^{*}=\delta_{i=j}=\sum_{k}t_{ki}^{*}t_{kj}\quad\text{ in }M_{\omega}.

In other words,

\lim_{n\to\omega}\sum_{k}t_{ik;n}t_{jk;n}^{*}=\delta_{i=j}=\lim_{n\to\omega}\sum_{k}t_{ki;n}^{*}t_{kj;n}.

Note that
(\tilde{\psi}_{\omega})_{g}=\operatorname{id}_{H}\otimes(\psi_{\omega})_{g}
,

\pi_{g}=t^{*}(\tilde{\psi}_{\omega})_{g}(t)=\sum_{i,j}\varepsilon_{ij}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{ki}^{*}(\psi_{\omega})_{g}(t_{kj})\in B(H)\otimes M_{\omega},

then

\pi_{g}(\eta_{j}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})=\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl}^{*}(\psi_{\omega})_{g}(t_{kj})b_{h}u_{h}=\lim_{n\to\omega}\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl;n}^{*}\psi_{g}(t_{kj;n})b_{h}u_{h}.

Let
x_{i}^{(n)}=\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}
and we shall show
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}_{i}
is an approximately inner
({}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G)_{M\rtimes G}
basis.

First, for
x=\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}\in{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
,

\displaystyle\left\|x-\sum_{i}x_{i}^{(n)}\langle x_{i}^{(n)}|x\rangle\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}\left\langle\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}\middle|\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}\right\rangle\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes\sum_{i}t_{ij;n}^{*}t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes\delta_{j=k}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

For
a=a_{g}u_{g}\in M\rtimes G
,

\displaystyle\left\|a\rhd x_{i}^{(n)}-x_{i}^{(n)}\lhd a\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|a_{g}u_{g}\rhd\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}\lhd a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\pi_{g}\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\sum_{j}\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl;n}^{*}\psi_{g}(t_{kj;n})a_{g}u_{g}t_{ij;n}^{*}u_{1}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}\sum_{j}t_{kl;n}^{*}\psi_{g}(t_{kj;n})a_{g}\psi_{g}(t_{ij;n}^{*})u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl;n}^{*}\sum_{j}\psi_{g}(t_{kj;n})\psi_{g}(t_{ij;n}^{*})a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl;n}^{*}\psi_{g}\left(\sum_{j}t_{kj;n}t_{ij;n}^{*}\right)a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\sum_{l}\eta_{l}\otimes\sum_{k}t_{kl;n}^{*}\delta_{k=i}a_{g}u_{g}-\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}a_{g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0

In the fifth approximation, we use the fact that
(t_{ij;n}^{*})_{n}
is a central sequence, so is
(\psi_{g}(t_{ij;n}^{*}))_{n}
.

Therefore,
{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
is approximately inner in
\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
.
∎

3.2.
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))
is centrally trivial

In this section, we show that
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is centrally trivial.
By unpacking the embedding
\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
, every representation
(H,\pi)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))
can be viewed as a
G
-representation, in particular
\pi_{g}=\operatorname{id}_{H}
if
\psi_{g}\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
.

The following proposition in Jones’ notes is essential.
We provide the proof for the convenience to the reader.

Proposition 3.10
.

[
Jon
]

Suppose
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner on a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.
Then if
(x_{n})\in M^{\omega}
with
\|x_{n}\alpha(y)-yx_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
for all
y\in M
,
then
\|x_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
.

Proof.

We prove the contrapositive.
Suppose there is a bounded sequence
(x_{n})
with
\|x_{n}\alpha(y)-yx_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
and
\|x_{n}\|_{2}>\delta>0
.
We can embed
M
in the
M_{2}(M^{\omega})
by

\sigma(y)=\begin{bmatrix}y&0\\
0&\alpha(y)\end{bmatrix}

For
x=(x_{n})\in M^{\omega}
, the condition
\|x_{n}\alpha(y)-yx_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
for all
y\in M
can be interpreted as

\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
x&0\end{bmatrix}\in\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega}).

We claim that
\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega})
is a
\rm II_{1}
-factor in this case.
Suppose
\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\in Z(\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega}))
,
then
\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}
must commute with
\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}
and
\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
,
so
b=c=0
.
Moreover,
since
\begin{bmatrix}a&0\\
0&d\end{bmatrix}
commutes with
\sigma(M)
,
a
and
d
are in
M_{\omega}=M^{\prime}\cap M^{\omega}
.
Since
M_{\omega}\subseteq\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega})
,
a
and
d

must be in
Z(M_{\omega})\cong{\mathbb{C}}
, then
a=\lambda
,
b=\theta
for some scalars
\lambda,\theta
.
Since
\begin{bmatrix}\lambda&0\\
0&\theta\end{bmatrix}
commutes with
\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
x&0\end{bmatrix}
, so
\lambda=\theta
.
Therefore,
\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega})
is a
\rm II_{1}
-factor and hence projections

\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}
and
\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
are equivalent.
This gives a unitary
U\in M^{\omega}
with
\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
U&0\end{bmatrix}\in\sigma(M)^{\prime}\cap M_{2}(M^{\omega})
and

\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\operatorname{Ad}u_{n}=\alpha
for
U=(u_{n})
,
Therefore,
\alpha
is approximately inner.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

Suppose the outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner, then

(M\rtimes G)_{\omega}=(M_{\omega})^{G}.

Proof.

Suppose
(a_{n})\in(M_{\omega})^{G}
, for
mu_{g}\in M\rtimes G
,

\displaystyle\|a_{n}mu_{g}-mu_{g}a_{n}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(a_{n}m-m\psi_{g}(a_{n}))u_{g}\|_{2}=\|a_{n}m-m\psi_{g}(a_{n})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a_{n}m-ma_{n}\|_{2}+\|m(a_{n}-\psi_{g}(a_{n})\|_{2}\to 0

the first term goes to 0 since
(a_{n})\in M_{\omega}
, the second term goes to 0 since
(a_{n})\in(M_{\omega})^{G}
.

For the other direction, suppose
(b_{n})\in(M\rtimes G)_{\omega}
,
b_{n}=\sum\limits_{g}b_{n,g}u_{g}
and
(b_{n,g})_{n}\in M^{\omega}
.
For
m\in M
,

\displaystyle\|b_{n}m-mb_{n}\|_{2}=\left\|\sum_{g}b_{n,g}u_{g}m-mb_{n,g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\sum_{g}(b_{n,g}\psi_{g}(m)-mb_{n,g})u_{g}\right\|_{2}\to 0

This implies
\|b_{n,g}\psi_{g}(m)-mb_{n,g}\|_{2}\to 0
for each
g\in G
and any
m\in M
. By Proposition
3.10
, since
\psi_{g}
is not approximately inner for all
g\neq e
, the bounded sequence
\|b_{n,g}\|_{2}\to 0
.
For
g\in G
,

\displaystyle\|b_{n,e}u_{g}-u_{g}b_{n,e}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(b_{n,e}-\psi_{g}(b_{n,e}))u_{g}\|_{2}\to 0,

which follows that
\|b_{n,e}-\psi_{g}(b_{n,e})\|_{2}\to 0
,
then

\displaystyle\|(b_{n,e}-E_{M^{G}}(b_{n,e})\|_{2}=\left\|b_{n,e}-\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g}\psi_{g}(b_{n,e})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g}\left\|b_{n,e}-\psi_{g}(b_{n,e})\right\|_{2}\to 0

Therefore
(b_{n})=(b_{n,e})=(E_{M^{G}}(b_{n,e}))=E_{(M_{\omega})^{G}}((b_{n,e}))\in(M_{\omega})^{G}
.
∎

Theorem 3.12
.

\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
is centrally trivial.

Proof.

Let
(H,\pi)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G/G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)))
and
{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
is the corresponding bimodule in
\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
.
We shall show that for each central sequence
(x_{n})\subseteq M\rtimes G
and
\xi\in{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
,
\|x_{n}\rhd\xi-\xi\lhd x_{n}\|\to 0
.

We write
x_{n}=\sum_{g}x_{n,g}u_{g}
. For each
g\in G
,
(x_{n,g})
is a bounded sequence and since

\left\|\left(\sum_{g}x_{n,g}u_{g}\right)a-a\left(\sum_{g}x_{n,g}u_{g}\right)\right\|_{2}\to 0

for every
a\in M
,
we have
\|x_{n,g}\psi_{g}(a)-ax_{n,g}\|_{2}\to 0
.
According to Proposition
3.10
,
\|x_{n,g}\|_{2}\to 0
if
\psi_{g}\notin{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
.

For
\xi=\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k}\in{}_{\pi}H\otimes M\rtimes G
, we have

\displaystyle\|x_{n}\rhd\xi-\xi\lhd x_{n}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{g\in G}x_{n,g}u_{g}\rhd(\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k})-(\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k})\lhd\sum_{g\in G}x_{n,g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{g\in G}\pi_{g}(\eta)\otimes x_{n,g}u_{g}a_{k}u_{k}-\sum_{g\in G}\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k}x_{n,g}u_{g}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\sum_{h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))}\pi_{h}(\eta)\otimes x_{n,h}u_{h}a_{k}u_{k}-\sum_{h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))}\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k}x_{n,h}u_{h}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))}\eta\otimes x_{n,h}u_{h}a_{k}u_{k}-\sum_{h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))}\eta\otimes a_{k}u_{k}x_{n,h}u_{h}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta\otimes\sum_{h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))}(x_{n,h}u_{h}a_{k}u_{k}-a_{k}u_{k}x_{n,h}u_{h})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx\left\|\eta\otimes\sum_{g\in G}(x_{n,g}u_{g}a_{k}u_{k}-a_{k}u_{k}x_{n,g}u_{g})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta\otimes(x_{n}a_{k}u_{k}-a_{k}u_{k}x_{n})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\approx 0

In the third and sixth approximation, we use the fact that
\|x_{n,g}\|_{2}\to 0
for
\psi_{g}\notin{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
.
The fourth equality holds since
\pi_{h}=\operatorname{id}_{H}
for
h\in G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))
.
The last approximation holds since
(x_{n})_{n}\subseteq M\rtimes G
is a central sequence.
∎

3.3.
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)\subseteq\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
is a unitary braided tensor subcategory

Let
K=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
and
L=G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))
.
Note that
[\operatorname{Ct}(M),{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)]\subseteq\operatorname{Int}(M)
, and
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is outer action,
K
and
L
commute in
G
.
Let
KL
be the smallest group generated by
K
and
L
. Since
K
and
L
are normal subgroups in
G
,
KL
is also a normal subgroup.
In Theorem
3.9
and Theorem
3.12
, we showed that
\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)\subseteq{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes G)
and
\mathsf{Rep}(G/L)\subseteq{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes G)
are full tensor subcategories.
Then
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
, as a full tensor subcategory of both
\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)
and
\mathsf{Rep}(G/L)
, is a full tensor subcategory of
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes G)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes G)=\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.

In this subsection, we moreover show that
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
is a unitary braided subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.

Denote
N=M\rtimes G
. For
(H,\pi),(V,\sigma)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G)
, we will show
the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({}_{\pi}H\otimes N)\boxtimes_{N}({}_{\sigma}V\otimes N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({}_{\sigma}V\otimes N)\boxtimes_{N}({}_{\pi}H\otimes N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\pi\otimes\sigma}(H\otimes V)\otimes N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{\sigma\otimes\pi}(V\otimes H)\otimes N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{{}_{\pi}H\otimes N,{}_{\sigma}V\otimes N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{2}_{(H,\pi),(V,\sigma)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\beta_{(H,\pi),(V,\sigma)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(F^{2}_{(V,\sigma),(H,\pi)}\right)^{\dagger}}

Let
\{\eta_{i}\}
and
\{\xi_{j}\}
be ONB for
H
and
V
respectively.
From Theorem
3.9
, we can take
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}_{i}
as approximately inner
({}_{\pi}H\otimes N)_{N}
-basis with
x_{i}^{(n)}=\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\otimes t_{ij;n}^{*}
where
t\in U(B(H)\otimes M_{\omega})
and
\pi_{g}=t^{*}\tilde{\psi}_{g}(t)
.
We take
\{\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1}\}_{j}
as the
({}_{\sigma}V\otimes N)_{N}
-basis.

For
(\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\boxtimes(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})\in({}_{\pi}H\otimes N)\boxtimes_{N}({}_{\sigma}V\otimes N)
, on one hand,

\displaystyle u_{{}_{\pi}H\otimes N,{}_{\sigma}K\otimes N}

\displaystyle[(\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\boxtimes(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})]

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\left\langle x_{i}^{(n)}\boxtimes(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\middle|(\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\boxtimes(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\left\langle\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1}\middle|\left\langle x_{i}^{(n)}\middle|(\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\right\rangle\rhd(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\left\langle\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1}\middle|t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}\rhd(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\left\langle\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1}\middle|\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}\langle\xi_{j}|\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\rangle t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(\xi_{j}\langle\xi_{j}|\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\rangle\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes x_{i}^{(n)}t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{i}(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes\left(\sum_{q}\eta_{q}\otimes t_{iq;n}^{*}\right)t_{ik;n}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle=(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes\sum_{q}\eta_{q}\otimes\left(\sum_{i}t_{iq;n}^{*}t_{ik;n}\right)a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle\approx(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes\sum_{q}\eta_{q}\otimes\delta_{q=k}a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}

\displaystyle=(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left(F^{2}_{(K,\sigma),(H,\pi)}\right)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\circ F(\beta_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)})\circ F^{2}_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)}[(\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g})\boxtimes(\xi_{l}\otimes b_{h}u_{h})]

\displaystyle=\left(F^{2}_{(K,\sigma),(H,\pi)}\right)^{\dagger}\circ F(\beta_{(H,\pi),(K,\sigma)})[(\eta_{k}\otimes\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l}))\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}]

\displaystyle=\left(F^{2}_{(K,\sigma),(H,\pi)}\right)^{\dagger}[(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes\eta_{k})\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}]

\displaystyle=(\sigma_{g}(\xi_{l})\otimes u_{1})\boxtimes\eta_{k}\otimes a_{g}u_{g}b_{h}u_{h}.

The results agree.
Therefore,
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
is a unitary braided subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.

However,
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
is not a central subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
in general.
We will give the argument in the appendix.

Theorem 3.13
.

Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is a finite group outer action on a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.
Then
\mathsf{Rep}(G/KL)
is a unitary braided full subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
, where
K=G\cap\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
and
L=G\cap\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))
.

4.
The generalization of Connes’ short exact sequence

In this section, we first give a computable formula for the deequivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.
Then we prove that the gauging construction is a honest categorical generalization of the Connes’ short exact sequence for the ordinary
\chi(M\rtimes G)
.

4.1.
The gauging construction

The gauging construction for braided tensor categories containing
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
is well-established in the literature. We refer to
[
Müg05
]
for the formulation in terms of
G
-crossed braided tensor categories arising from orbifold conformal field theories, and to
[
ENO05
,
DGNO10
]
for the categorical theory of equivariantization and de-equivariantization of fusion categories.

Suppose
G
is a finite group, a
G
-crossed braided tensor category
is a
G
-graded tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}=\bigoplus_{g\in G}{\mathcal{C}}_{g}
equipped with an action
T:G\to\operatorname{Aut}_{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
,
g\mapsto T_{g}
satisfying
T_{g}({\mathcal{C}}_{h})\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{ghg^{-1}}
, together with a
G
-crossed braiding

c_{x,y}:x\otimes y\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{g}(y)\otimes x,\qquad\text{ for }x\in{\mathcal{C}}_{g},

satisfying the coherences.

Now let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a unitary braided tensor category containing
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as a braided full tensor subcategory, and let
\mathsf{Fun}(G)\in\mathsf{Rep}(G)
be a commutative Q-system (unital unitary separable Frobenius algebra), and
A
be the image of
\mathsf{Fun}(G)
in
{\mathcal{B}}
.
The (
G
-)deequivariantization
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
is the category
{\mathcal{B}}_{A}
of right
A
-modules in
{\mathcal{B}}
.
This category carries a canonical structure of a
G
-crossed braided tensor category.
Concretely, if
X\in{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
is indecomposable with right
A
-action
\rho_{X}:X\otimes A\to X
, then the braiding in
{\mathcal{B}}
induces a left
A
-action

\lambda_{X}:=\rho_{X}\circ\beta^{-1}_{A,X}:A\otimes X\to X.

For indecomposable
X
, there exists a unique
\partial_{X}\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)\cong\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(\mathsf{Fun}(G))\cong G
such that

\lambda_{X}\circ(\partial_{X}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{X})=\rho_{X}\circ\beta_{A,X}

The assignment
X\mapsto\partial_{X}
defines the homogeneous degree of
X
, hence a grading

{\mathcal{B}}_{G}=\bigoplus_{g\in G}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{g}.

The tensor product over
A
is compatible with this grading
\partial_{X\otimes_{A}Y}=\partial_{X}\partial_{Y}
, and together with the induced
G
-action and crossed braiding.
For
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{A}
with right action
\rho_{Y}:Y\otimes A\to Y
and
g\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)
,

T_{g}(Y):=(Y,\rho_{Y}^{g}),\qquad\text{where }\rho_{Y}^{g}=\rho_{Y}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{Y}\otimes g).

Then for
X\in{\mathcal{B}}_{A}
with grading
\partial_{X}
,
the
G
-crossed braiding
c_{X,Y}:X\otimes_{A}Y\to T_{\partial_{X}}(Y)\otimes_{A}X
is given by

c_{X,Y}:=\left(p_{X,T_{g}(Y)}^{A}\right)^{\dagger}\circ\beta_{X,Y}\circ p_{X,Y}^{A},

where
p_{X,Y}:X\otimes Y\to X\otimes_{A}Y
is the projection given by the coequalizer
X\otimes A\otimes Y\rightrightarrows X\otimes Y
.

Conversely, if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
G
-crossed braided tensor category, one may form its
G
-equivariantization
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}
, whose objects are pairs
(x,\{u_{g}\}_{g\in G})
consisting of an object
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
together with coherent isomorphisms
u_{g}:T_{g}(x)\xrightarrow{\sim}x
.
The
G
-equivariantization
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}
is again a braided tensor category, and it contains
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as a braided full tensor subcategory.
Deequivariantization and equivariantization establish a fundamental correspondence:
If
{\mathcal{B}}
is a braided fusion category containing
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as a braided fusion subcategory, then its deequivariantization
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
is a
G
-crossed braided fusion category.
Conversely, if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
G
-crossed braided fusion category, then its equivariantization
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}
is a braided fusion category containing
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
These processes are inverses up to equivalence:
({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}\cong{\mathcal{B}}
and
({\mathcal{C}}^{G})_{G}\cong{\mathcal{C}}
.

This correspondence provides an equivalence between:

\left\{\parbox{128.0374pt}{\rm\centering Braided tensor categories \\
containing $\mathsf{Rep}(G)$ \\
as braided subcategory\@add@centering}\right\}
\left\{\parbox{99.58464pt}{\centering$G$-crossed braided \\
tensor categories\@add@centering}\right\}
deequivariantization
G
-equivariantization

From the viewpoint of physics, this pair of constructions is the categorical form of gauging a finite global symmetry in a
2+1
-dimensional topological phase.
A braided fusion category describes the anyons of the ungauged phase, while a
G
-crossed braided extension incorporates extrinsic symmetry defects labeled by group elements
g\in G
.
Passing from the defect theory to the equivariantized category corresponds to promoting the global symmetry
G
to a local gauge symmetry: the formerly extrinsic
G
-defects become dynamical excitations of the gauged theory. Conversely, deequivariantization may be viewed as “ungauging,” in which one identifies a Tannakian
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
sector and passes back to the defect-enriched theory. This gauging paradigm plays a central role in the modern description of symmetry-enriched topological phases, symmetry defects, see e.g.
[
BBCW19
]
.

4.2.
Graphical calculus and realization

In order to compute the deequivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
, we use the graphical calculus.
We refer readers the graphical calculus and realization to
[
CHPJP22
]
.
For the part of the graphical calculus we need here, we refer the section 4 and section 5 from
[
CJP24
]
.

We use the 2D graphical calculus on
\mathsf{Bim}(M)
.
We denote
{}_{{\mathbb{C}}}M_{M}
by a dashed line with shading on the right hand side of the line by
M
.

uses the convention in Notation

By using the regular basis and approximately inner basis, the braiding in
\tilde{\chi}(M)
can be defined as the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-limits of
M
-finite rank operators, namely:
Suppose
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
and
\{y_{j}^{(n)}\}
are approximately inner
X_{M}
and
Y_{M}
basis,
\{x_{i}\}
and
\{y_{j}\}
are regular
X_{M}
and
Y_{M}
basis respectively, then the unitary braiding in
\tilde{\chi}(M)
are given by

Suppose
M
is a
\rm II_{1}
factor.
A Q-system in
\mathsf{Bim}(M)
is a triple
(Q,M,i)
, where
Q\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)
, the multiplication
m:Q\boxtimes_{M}Q\to Q
and the unit
i:M\to Q
are bounded bimodule intertwiners such that
Q
is a unitary separable Frobenius algebra.
Its realization

|Q|:=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{C}}-M}({}_{{\mathbb{C}}}M_{M}\to{}_{{\mathbb{C}}}M\boxtimes_{M}Q_{M}),

is a von Neumann algebra containing
M
with a conditional expectation
E_{M}:|Q|\to M
.
When
Q
is connected, i.e.,
\dim(\operatorname{Hom}_{M-M}(M\to Q))=1
, then
|Q|
is also a
\rm II_{1}
factor.

Definition 4.1

(
[
CHPJP22
, §4.2]
)
.

Suppose
Q\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)
is a Q-system and
X
is a
Q
-
Q
bimodule.
The realization
|X|:=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{C}}-M}({}_{\mathbb{C}}M_{M}\to{}_{\mathbb{C}}M\boxtimes_{M}X_{M})
of
X
is a
|Q|
-
|Q|
bimodule
with
|Q|
-
|Q|
bimodule action and right
|Q|
-valued inner product respectively by

Hence
|X|\in\mathsf{Bim}(|Q|)
is a
\rm W^{*}
-correspondence.

Remark 4.2
.

Given a group action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
, this induces a fully faithful tensor functor
F:\mathsf{Hilb}(G)\to\mathsf{Bim}(M)
by
g\mapsto{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)
.
Let
Q={\mathbb{C}}[G]\in\mathsf{Hilb}(G)
.
We know that the category of
Q
-
Q
bimodules in
\mathsf{Hilb}(G)
is tensor equivalent to
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
Therefore, the realization
|\cdot|_{F}
induces a tensor embedding
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\cong\mathsf{Bim}(Q)\to\mathsf{Bim}(|Q|)=\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes G)
. This functor coincides with the one described at the beginning of section
3
.

Facts 4.3
.

Suppose
Q\in\tilde{\chi}(M)
is a commutative Q-system and
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|Q|)
.
The following facts based on
[
CJP24
, Prop. 5.10 & 5.13]
hold:

(1)

If
\{q_{j}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|Q|_{M}
basis, then
(q_{j}^{(n)})_{n}
is a central sequence in
|Q|
.

(2)

Suppose
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{|Q|}
basis, then

\{c_{i,j}^{(n)}\}=\{x_{i}^{(n)}\lhd q_{j}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{M}
basis.

(3)

Suppose
\{x_{i}\}
is an
|X|_{|Q|}
basis, and
\{q_{j}\}
is a
|Q|_{M}
basis, then
\{c_{i,j}\}=\{x_{i}\lhd q_{j}\}
is a
|X|_{M}
basis.

(4)

If
\{x_{i}\}
is an
X_{M}
basis, then
\{x_{i}\}
is also an
|X|_{|Q|}
basis; if
\{x_{i}\}
is an approximate
X_{M}
basis, then
\{x_{i}\}
is also an approximate
|X|_{|Q|}
basis.

4.3.
Deequivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)

\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is outer and
\psi
is not approximately inner.

3.13
,
\mathsf{Rep}(G/K)
is a unitary braided fully subcategory of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
.

A

We first show that the realization gives a fully faithful embedding from the deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
to
\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
.

Proposition 4.4
.

If the bimodule
X
a right
A
-module in
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes G)
,
then
|X|\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
.

Proof.

Since
X
is a right
A
-module and
X
can be equipped with a left
A
-module structure,
X
is an
A
-
A
bimodule.
The the realization
|X|
is an
|A|
-
|A|
bimodule.

\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is an approximate inner

-basis,

is an
A_{M\rtimes G}

The second equality uses the definition of left action (
4.1
);
the third approximation is from the definition of the under braiding
u_{X,A}^{\dagger}
;
the forth approximation is from the fact that
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is approximately inner
X_{M\rtimes G}
basis and
\langle q_{k}\mid q\rangle\in M\rtimes G
;
the fifth approximation is because
\left(\langle x_{j}^{(n)}\mid x_{i}^{(n)}\rangle\right)
is a central sequence in
(M\rtimes G)_{\omega}
and
|Q|
is centrally trivial.

Therefore
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{|A|}
basis.
∎

For
g\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)
, we denote
g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)
to the category of
|A|
-
|A|
bimodules
(X,\lambda^{g}_{X})
, where
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)
with the same right
|A|
-action and a new left
|A|
-action
\lambda^{g}_{X}:=\lambda_{X}\circ(g\otimes\operatorname{id}_{X}):|A|\boxtimes_{|A|}X\to X
.

Proposition 4.5
.

If bimodule
X
is an indecomposable right
A
-module in
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes G)
,
then
|X|\in{}_{\partial X}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)
.

Proof.

Suppose
\{x_{j}\}
is an
X_{M\rtimes G}
-basis and
\{q_{k}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
A_{M\rtimes G}
-basis.
For every central sequence
(q_{n})\in|Q|_{\omega}
and
x\in|X|
.

q_{n}\rhd_{\partial_{X}}x=\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to43.28pt{\vbox to135.43pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 29.05316pt\lower-20.11684pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

The first equality uses the definition of left action (
4.1
);
the second equality is by (
4.1
);
the third approximation is from the definition of the over braiding
u_{A,X}
;
the forth approximation is from the fact that
(\langle q_{k}^{(n)}\mid q_{n}\rangle)
is a central sequence and
X
is centrally trivial;
the fifth approximation is because
\{q_{k}^{(n)}\}
is approximately inner
|Q|_{M\rtimes G}
basis and
\langle x_{j}\mid x\rangle\in M\rtimes G
.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Realization gives a
G/K
-crossed braided unitary equivalence

|\cdot|:\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{A}\to\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|).

Proof.

By proposition
4.4
and
4.5
, the realization functor is fully faithful.
Especially,
|X|\in g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
when
\partial_{X}=g
.

We now show that the realization functor is essentially surjective, i.e, every bimodule in
\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
is unitary isomorphic to a realization of a bimodule in
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{A}
.
It is suffices to consider an indecomposable
A
-
A
bimodule
X\in\mathsf{Bim}(M)
such that
|X|\in g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
for some
g\in G/K
.

By Facts
4.3
(2), suppose
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{|A|}
-basis and
\{q_{j}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|A|_{M\rtimes G}
-basis, then
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\lhd q_{j}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{M}
-basis.

The first approximation is from the definition of the under braiding
u_{X,A}^{{\dagger}}
;
the second approximation is from Fact
4.3
(1) that
(q_{j}^{(n)})
is a central sequence in
|A|
;
the third approximation is because
\{x_{i}^{(n)}\}
is an approximately inner
|X|_{|A|}
basis so that
\|q_{k}x_{i}^{(n)}-x_{i}^{(n)}q_{k}\|_{2}\to 0
.

Similarly, suppose
\{x_{i}\}
is an
|X|_{|A|}
-basis and
\{q_{j}\}
is an
|A|_{M\rtimes G}
-basis, then by Facts
4.3
(3)
\{x_{i}\lhd q_{j}\}
is an
|X|_{M}
-basis.
