The first approximation is from the definition of the over braiding
u_{A,X}
;
the second approximation is from Facts
4.3
(1) that
(q_{k}^{(n)})
is a central sequence in
|A|
;
the third approximation is because
X
is
g
-centrally trivial over
|A|
and
(q_{k}^{(n)})
is a central sequence in
|A|
so that
\|q_{k}^{(n)}\rhd_{g}x_{i}-x_{i}\lhd q_{k}^{(n)}\|_{2}\to 0
.

Because
g\in\operatorname{Aut}(A)
is an algebra automorphism, we have
g\circ i=i
, where
i:1\to A
is the unit.
For central sequence
(a_{n})\in(M\rtimes G)_{\omega}
, since
A
is centrally trivial, for all
q\in A
,
\|a_{n}q-qa_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
, so
(a_{n})
is also a central sequence on
|A|
.
Then for
x\in|X|
,

\|a_{n}\rhd x-x\lhd a_{n}\|_{2}=\|a_{n}\rhd_{g}x-x\lhd a_{n}\|_{2}\to 0,

so
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes G)
.

Moreover, combining above equations, if
|X|\in g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
,
X
is a right
A
-module in
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
and and the realization preserve the grading
\partial_{X}=g
.

Similar to the proof of
[
CJP24
, Thm. 5.15]
, the realization gives a
G/K
-crossed braided equivalence
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{A}\to\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

In
[
CJP24
, Thm. 5.15]
, they prove that for commutative Q-system
Q\in\tilde{\chi}(M)
, the local extension
\tilde{\chi}(M)_{Q}^{\operatorname{loc}}
is braided equivalent to
\tilde{\chi}(|Q|)
.
In our case,
A
is a commutative Q-system in
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
, then the local extension
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{A}^{\operatorname{loc}}
is braided equivalent to
\tilde{\chi}(|A|)={\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|Q|)
.
Note that in the group case, the local extension is the
e
-grading part of the deequivariantization.
By comparing the formulas, we can see
\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)
, which is equivalent to the deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
, is a complete
G/K
-crossed braided extension of
\tilde{\chi}(|A|)
.

In the following, we use the Morita equivalence of the basic construction
M\rtimes K\subseteq M\rtimes G\subseteq|A|
to give a more computable formula.

For
g\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)
, we define an
|A|
-
|A|
bimodule
gL^{2}(|A|)
by twisting the left action by
\lambda^{g}:=\lambda\circ(g\otimes\operatorname{id}_{L^{2}(|A|)})
.
Since
\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)\cong G/K
, this gives an action

\Psi:G/K\to\mathsf{Bim}(|A|)\qquad\text{by}\qquad g\mapsto gL^{2}(|A|).

Note that
M\rtimes K
and
|A|
are Morita equivalent by the bimodule
Z={}_{M\rtimes G}L^{2}(M\rtimes G)_{|A|}
such that

Z\boxtimes_{|A|}\overline{Z}\cong\operatorname{id}_{L^{2}(M\rtimes K)}\in\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes K),\qquad\qquad\overline{Z}\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}Z\cong\operatorname{id}_{L^{2}(|A|)}\in\mathsf{Bim}(|A|).

Then

\operatorname{Ad}(Z):\mathsf{Bim}(|A|)\to\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes K)\qquad\text{by}\qquad X\mapsto Z\boxtimes_{|A|}X\boxtimes_{|A|}\overline{Z}

is a tensor equivalence.

Then using the Morita equivalence, we can define an induced action, without loss of generality, still denoted as
\psi:G/K\to\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes K)
by

\psi:=\operatorname{Ad}(Z)\circ\Psi.

Then by the construction, for
g\in G/K
, we have

Z\boxtimes_{|A|}gL^{2}(P)\boxtimes_{|A|}\overline{Z}\cong{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\in\mathsf{Bim}(M\rtimes K).

Theorem 4.8
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
M
is a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor.
Suppose
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is an outer action and
\psi
is not approximately inner.
Then the
G/K
-deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G/K}\cong\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)

as unitary
G/K
-crossed braided tensor categories, where
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))
.

Proof.

According to
[
CJP24
, Thm. A]
,
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\tilde{\chi}(N)
if
M
and
N
are Morita equivalent.
In our case,
\operatorname{Ad}(Z):{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\to{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K)
and
\operatorname{Ad}(Z):{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)\to{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)
are tensor equivalences, and
\operatorname{Ad}(Z)
is the braided equivalence on
\tilde{\chi}
.
Therefore, recall the construction of
g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)
, a centrally trivial bimodule twists the left action by
g\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{alg}}(A)
, the Morita equivalence gives an equivalence

\operatorname{Ad}(Z):g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\to{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K).

Recall the
G/K
-crossed braiding construction (
4.1
),
the Morita equivalence gives a
G/K
-crossed braided equivalence

\operatorname{Ad}(Z):\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}g{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(|A|)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(|A|)\to\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)

∎

4.4.
Gauging and short exact sequence

In this section, we will show that the equivariantization/deequivariantization procedure induces a short exact sequence with the first entry being
\widehat{G}
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a rigid
\rm C^{*}
tensor category with simple unit
1_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\rho
is a (strict)
G
-action on
{\mathcal{C}}
by tensor autoequivalences
\rho:G\to\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
.
A
G
-equivariant structure
on object
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
is a family of unitary isomorphisms
\{u_{g}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\rho_{g}(x)\to x)\}_{g\in G}
such that

u_{gh}=u_{g}\circ\rho_{g}(u_{h}).

Lemma 4.9
.

The group of
G
-equivariant structures on the tensor unit
1_{\mathcal{C}}
is isomorphic to
\widehat{G}=\operatorname{Hom}(G\to U(1))
.

The proof of this lemma follows from the facts that
\rho_{g}(1_{\mathcal{C}})\cong 1_{{\mathcal{C}}}
and
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{C}}(1_{\mathcal{C}})\cong{\mathbb{C}}
directly.

Proposition 4.10
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
{\mathcal{B}}
is a braided
\rm C^{*}
tensor category and
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}
is a unitary braided fully faithful tensor subcategory. Then

0\to\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}})\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}\to 0

is a short exact sequence, where

\operatorname{Inv}(-)
is group of isomorphism classes of invertible objects and

\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}=\{[y]\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})\mid y\ \mathrm{admits\ a\ unitary\ }G\mathrm{-equivariant\ structure}\}

Proof.

There is a canonical tensor functor to the deequivariantization
F:{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
by
X\mapsto X\otimes A
such that

F(x)\otimes_{A}F(y)=(x\otimes A)\otimes_{A}(y\otimes A)\cong(x\otimes y)\otimes A=F(x\otimes y).

If
x\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}})
with inverse
x^{*}
, i.e.,
x\otimes x^{*}\cong 1_{{\mathcal{B}}}\cong x^{*}\otimes x
, then
F(x)=x\otimes A
is invertible in
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
with inverse
x^{*}\otimes A
.
Therefore,
F
induces a group homomorphism

\Pi:\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}})\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G}),\qquad\quad[x]\mapsto[x\otimes A].

By the equivariantization/deequivariantization correspondence,
{\mathcal{B}}\cong({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}
, the
G
-equivariant objects in
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
.
Under this equivalence,
F
is the forgetful functor that drops the
G
-equivariant structure (only remembers the underlying
A
-modules).
Therefore, the image of
\Pi
consists exactly of those invertible objects of
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
that admits a
G
-equivariant structure.
Define

\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}=\{[y]\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})\mid y\ \mathrm{admits\ a\ unitary\ }G\mathrm{-equivariant\ structure}\}

Then the image of
\Pi
is in
\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}
.

Every character
\gamma\in\widehat{G}
gives a 1-dimensional
G
-representation
{\mathbb{C}}_{\gamma}
, which is an invertible object in
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
Then we define

\delta:\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}),\qquad\quad\gamma\mapsto[{\mathbb{C}}_{\gamma}].

It is clearly a group homomorphism since
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
is monoidal.

Now we show exactness.

•

\delta
is injective.

Since
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
is a full tensor subcategory,
\delta
is injective.

•

\operatorname{im}(\delta)\subseteq\ker(\Pi)
.

For
\gamma\in\widehat{G}
, we know that
{\mathbb{C}}_{\gamma}\otimes A\cong A
as
G
-representations. Moreover,
{\mathbb{C}}_{\gamma}\otimes A\cong A
as right
A
-modules. Therefore,
\Pi(\delta(\gamma))=[A]
.

•

\ker(\Pi)\subseteq\operatorname{im}(\delta)
.

Suppose
[x]\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}})
with
\Pi([x])=[A]
, which means
x\otimes A\cong A
as right
A
-modules in
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
.
Now use the equivalence
{\mathcal{B}}\cong({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}
.
Under this equivalence,
x
corresponds to a
G
-equivariant object whose underlying
A
-module is
x\otimes A\cong A
.
According to Lemma
4.9
, when
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{B}}_{G}
, its tensor unit is
A
as right
A
-module, then the group of
G
-equivariant structure on
A
is isomorphic to
\widehat{G}
. This gives
\ker(\Pi)\subseteq\operatorname{im}(\delta)
.

•

\Pi
is surjective.

Let
[y]\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})_{\rm{lift}}
.
We choose a unitary
G
-equivariant structure
u
on
y
, then
(y,u)\in({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}
.
Since
y
is invertible in
{\mathcal{B}}
,
(y,u)
is invertible in
({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}
.
Under the equivalence
{\mathcal{B}}\cong({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{G}
, there is an invertible object
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
corresponds to
(y,u)
.
By forgetting the
G
-equivariant structure, we have
\Pi([x])=[y]
.
In other words, every liftable
y
lands in the image of
\Pi
, so
\Pi
is surjective. ∎

4.5.
Invertible liftable objects

To complete the story, we shall show the invertible liftable objects in the
G/K
-deequivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
is actually equivalent to
(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M)
, the third term from the Connes’ short exact sequence.

Lemma 4.11
.

Suppose
M
is a
\rm II_{1}
factor,
A\subseteq M
is a finite von Neumann subalgebra with a trace-preserving conditional expectation
E_{A}:M\to A
.
Let
u\in U(M)
,
\varepsilon>0
is small, and
a\in(A)_{1}
.
If
\|u-a\|_{2}<\varepsilon
, then there exists a unitary
v\in A
such that
\|u-v\|_{2}<\varepsilon+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}
.
In particular, if
(a_{n})\subseteq(A)_{1}
such that
\|u-a_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
, then one can choose
v_{n}\in U(A)
such that
\|u-v_{n}\|_{2}\to 0
.

Proof.

Write the polar decomposition of
a
in
A
as
a=q|a|
, where
q\in A
is a partial isometry.
Since
A
is a finite von Neumann algebra, the partial isometry
q
can extend to a unitary
v
and
a=v|a|
.

We now estimate
\|a-v\|_{2}
.
First we have

\|a-v\|_{2}^{2}=\|v|a|-v\|_{2}^{2}=\||a|-1\|_{2}=\operatorname{tr}((1-|a|)^{2})

Since
0\leq|a|\leq 1
and
(1-t)^{2}\leq 1-t^{2}
for
t\in[0,1]
, by functional calculus, we have

\|a-v\|_{2}^{2}=\operatorname{tr}((1-|a|)^{2})\leq\operatorname{tr}(1-|a|^{2})=1-\|a\|_{2}^{2}

From
\|u-a\|_{2}<\varepsilon
,
\|a\|_{2}>1-\varepsilon
, then
\|a\|_{2}^{2}>(1-\varepsilon)^{2}>1-2\varepsilon
. This implies

\|a-v\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1-\|a\|_{2}^{2}<2\varepsilon.

Therefore,

\|u-v\|_{2}\leq\|u-a\|_{2}+\|a-v\|_{2}<\varepsilon+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}.\qed

The following result is appeared in Jones’
\chi(M)
notes with a sketch proof.
Here we provide a complete proof.

Proposition 4.12
.

[
Jon
]

Suppose
M
is a separable
\rm II_{1}
factor,
A\subseteq M
is a subalgebra with conditional expectation
E_{A}:M\to A
such that for any central sequence
(a_{n})_{n}
in
M
,
\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\|E_{A}(a_{n})-a_{n}\|_{2}=0
.
Then if
\alpha\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
, there exist a unitary
w\in M
and a sequence of unitaries
(z_{n})_{n}
in
A
such that
\alpha=\operatorname{Ad}(w)\circ\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
.

Proof.

Recall that the pointwise topology on
\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is given by
\beta_{n}\to\beta
if
\|\beta_{n}(x)-\beta(x)\|_{2}\to 0
for all
x\in M
.
In other words, let
\delta>0
and a finite set
F\subseteq(M)_{1}
, the neighborhood
V_{F,\delta}(\beta)
of
\beta\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is given by

V_{F,\delta}(\beta)=\{\gamma\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)\mid\max_{x\in F}\|\gamma(x)-\beta(x)\|_{2}<\delta\}.

First we prove the following claim.

Lemma.
For every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist a finite set
F\subseteq(M)_{1}
and
\delta>0
such that whenever
u\in U(M)
and
\operatorname{Ad}(u)\in V_{F,\delta}(\operatorname{id})
, there exists
a\in(A)_{1}
such that
\|u-a\|_{2}<\varepsilon
.

Proof of Lemma
: By contradiction, suppose there is some
\varepsilon>0
, for every finite subset
F\subseteq(M)_{1}
and
\delta>0
, there is a unitary
u_{F,\delta}\in M
such that
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{F,\delta})\in V_{F,\delta}(\operatorname{id})
and
\|u_{F,\delta}-a\|_{2}\geq\varepsilon
for all
a\in(A)_{1}
.
Since
M
is separable, there is a countable dense subset
\{x_{m}\}\subseteq(M)_{1}
in
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-norm topology.
Let
F_{n}=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\}
and
\delta_{n}=\frac{1}{n}
, we obtain a sequence
(u_{n})
such that

•

\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n})\in V_{F_{n},\delta_{n}}(\operatorname{id})
, i.e.,
\|\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n})(x_{i})-x_{i}\|_{2}=\|[u_{n},x_{i}]\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
;

•

\|u_{n}-a\|_{2}\geq\varepsilon
for all
a\in(A)_{1}
.

Then
(u_{n})
is a central sequence in
M
: Indeed, for
x\in(M)_{1}
,

\displaystyle\|[u_{n},x]\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|[u_{n},x_{i}]+[u_{n},x-x_{i}]\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|[u_{n},x_{i}]\|_{2}+2\|u_{n}\|\|x-x_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|[u_{n},x_{i}]\|_{2}+2\|x-x_{i}\|_{2}

then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we pick
i
such that
\|x-x_{i}\|_{2}<\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
and pick
N\geq i
such that
\frac{1}{N}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, which follows that
\|[u_{n},x]\|_{2}<\varepsilon
for all
n\geq N
.
However, since
E_{A}(u_{n})\in(A)_{1}
, we have

\varepsilon\leq\liminf_{a\in(A)_{1}}\|u_{n}-a\|_{2}\leq\lim\|u_{n}-E_{A}(u_{n})\|_{2}=0,

which is a contradiction. This proves the lemma.

We can additionally refine the above
Lemma
to require
a\in(A)_{1}
to be unitary by Lemma
4.11
.

Now since
\alpha\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
, there exists a sequence of unitaries in
M
such that
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n})\to\alpha
in
\|\cdot\|_{2}
.
Note that
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n}^{*}u_{n+1})=\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n}^{*})\circ\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n+1})\to\alpha^{-1}\circ\alpha=\operatorname{id}
pointwise.
For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists a finite subset
F_{n}\subseteq(M)_{1}
and
\delta_{n}>0
such that
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{n}^{*}u_{n+1})\in V_{F_{n},\delta_{n}}(\operatorname{id})
.
Then by the refined claim, there exists a unitary
v_{n}\in A
such that
\|u_{n}^{*}u_{n+1}-v_{n}\|_{2}<2^{-n}
, equivalently speaking,
\|u_{n}v_{n}u_{n+1}^{*}-1\|_{2}<2^{-n}
.
Then
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}u_{n}v_{n}u_{n+1}^{*}
is a Cauchy sequence and we denote its limit as
t
.

Note that
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}=u_{1}^{*}\left(\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}u_{n}v_{n}u_{n+1}^{*}\right)u_{n+1}
. Then
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\operatorname{Ad}\left(\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\right)
exists and equals
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{1}^{*}t)\circ\alpha
.
Since
v_{i}\in U(A)
, we define the unitary
z_{n}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\in A
and
w=t^{*}u_{1}
, we have the desired result.
∎

Proposition 4.13
.

Suppose outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner,
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
.
The group of invertible
G/K
-liftable bimodules in
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)
is equivalent to the group of
G
-approximately inner automorphisms
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
modulo inner automorphisms, i.e.,

\operatorname{Inv}({\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K))_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M).

Proof.

Let
N=M\rtimes K
.
Suppose
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
, there exists unitaries
(z_{n})\subseteq M^{G}
such that
\alpha=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
.
Define
\widetilde{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Aut}(N)
by
\widetilde{\alpha}(au_{k})=\alpha(a)u_{k}
for
a\in M
,
k\in K
.
Since
z_{n}\in M^{G}\subseteq M^{K}
,
z_{n}
commutes with every
u_{k}
, we have
\widetilde{\alpha}=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
and the bimodule
{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N))
.
Because
\alpha
is implemented by
G
-fixed unitaries,

\psi_{g}\alpha\psi_{g}^{-1}(a)=\psi_{g}\lim z_{n}\psi_{g}^{-1}(a)z_{n}^{*}=\psi_{g}\lim\psi_{g}^{-1}(z_{n}az_{n}^{*})=\lim z_{n}az_{n}^{*}=\alpha(a),

for all
a\in M
,
g\in G
, so
\psi_{g}\alpha\psi_{g}^{-1}=\alpha
and consequently
\psi_{g}\widetilde{\alpha}\psi_{g}^{-1}=\widetilde{\alpha}
.
Thus the identity maps give a unitary
G/K
-equivariant structure on
{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)
showing it is liftable.

If
\alpha^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ad}(v)\circ\alpha
with
v\in U(M)
, then

\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ad}(v)\circ\widetilde{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Aut}(N)
, hence
{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}L^{2}(N)\cong{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)
.
So the construction depends only on the class of
\alpha
in
\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M)
.

Now we prove the converse. Suppose
Y\in\operatorname{Inv}({\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N))_{\mathsf{lift}}
, we shall show
Y\cong{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)
for some
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
.

Because
Y
is invertible and approximately inner,
Y\cong{}_{\beta}L^{2}(N)
for some
\beta\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(N)
.
Since
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner, so is its restriction on
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
, then by Corollary
3.11
,
(M\rtimes K)_{\omega}\cong(M_{\omega})^{K}
.
In other words, for every central sequence
(a_{n})\in N_{\omega}
,
\|a_{n}-E_{M^{K}}(a_{n})\|_{2}\to 0
.
By Proposition
4.12
, there exist unitaries
w\in N
and
z_{n}\in M^{K}
such that
\beta=\operatorname{Ad}(w)\circ\lim\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
.
Since we work with equivalence class of bimodules, we may replace
\beta
by
\alpha=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
, and
Y\cong{}_{\alpha}L^{2}(N)
as well.
Moreover, since
\alpha=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(z_{n})
,
z_{n}\in M^{K}
,
\alpha\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
.

In this case, since
z_{n}\in M^{K}
, we have
[z_{n},u_{k}]=0
for all
k\in K
, then

\alpha(u_{k})=\lim z_{n}u_{k}z_{n}^{*}=\lim u_{k}z_{n}z_{n}^{*}=u_{k}
.

The
G/K
-action on
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N)
is given by

\rho_{\bar{g}}(X):={}_{\psi_{\bar{g}}}X_{\psi_{\bar{g}}}=({}_{\psi_{\bar{g}}}L^{2}(N))\boxtimes_{N}X\boxtimes_{N}({}_{\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}}L^{2}(N)).

Since
Y\cong{}_{\alpha}L^{2}(N)
is liftable, there exists a unitary
G/K
-equivariant structure

v_{\bar{g}}\in\mathsf{Bim}(N)(\rho_{\bar{g}}(Y)\to Y),\qquad\bar{g}\in G/K,

such that
v_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}=v_{\bar{g}}\circ\rho_{\bar{g}}(v_{\bar{h}}).

Because
\rho_{\bar{g}}(Y)\cong{}_{\psi_{\bar{g}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}}L^{2}(N)
,
each
v_{\bar{g}}
is given by a unitary
t_{\bar{g}}\in U(N)
such that

\psi_{\bar{g}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}=\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{g}})\circ\alpha.

From (1), we have

\displaystyle\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\psi_{\bar{g}}(\psi_{\bar{h}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{h}}^{-1})\psi_{\bar{h}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\psi_{\bar{g}}(\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{h}})\circ\alpha)\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ad}(\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}}))\circ\psi_{\bar{g}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ad}(\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}}))\circ\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{g}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ad}(\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}})t_{\bar{g}})\circ\alpha

On the other hand,

\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}\alpha\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}^{-1}=\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{g}t_{\bar{h}}})\circ\alpha.

Comparing the two implementations, after normalizing scalars, we may assume

t_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}=\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}})t_{\bar{g}},\qquad\forall\bar{g},\bar{h}\in G/K.

Applying (1) to
a\in N
. Since

\psi_{\bar{g}}\beta\psi_{\bar{g}}^{-1}(a)=\psi_{\bar{g}}\!\left(\lim z_{n}\psi_{\bar{g}^{-1}}(a)z_{n}^{*}\right)=\lim\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n})a\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n})^{*},

and

\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{g}})\circ\beta(a)=\lim t_{\bar{g}}z_{n}az_{n}^{*}t_{\bar{g}}^{*},

we get

\|\operatorname{Ad}(\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n}))(a)-\operatorname{Ad}(t_{\bar{g}}z_{n})(a)\|_{2}\to 0\qquad\forall a\in N.

so for
c_{\bar{g};n}:=z_{n}^{*}t_{\bar{g}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n})\in N
,
we have
(c_{\bar{g};n})\in N_{\omega}=(M_{\omega})^{K}
.
Then

\|t_{\bar{g}}^{*}-z_{n}c_{\bar{g};n}\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n}^{*})\|_{2}=\|z_{n}^{*}t_{\bar{g}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n})-c_{\bar{g};n}\|_{2}\to 0.

Note that
\psi_{\bar{g}}
preserves
M^{K}
, then
z_{n}^{*},\ \psi_{\bar{g}}(z_{n})\in M^{K}
.
Since
c_{\bar{g}}=(c_{\bar{g};n})\in(M_{\omega})^{K}
, and
M^{K}
is closed in
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-norm,
we have
t_{\bar{g}}\in M^{K}
.

Moreover,
\{c_{\bar{g}}\}_{\bar{g}\in G/K}
is a
1
-cocycle for the induced action
\psi_{\omega}:G/K\to\operatorname{Aut}(M_{\omega})
.
Indeed, writing
z=(z_{n})_{n}\in U(M^{\omega})
, using
(2)
, we compute

\displaystyle c_{\bar{g}}\psi_{\bar{g}}(c_{\bar{h}})

\displaystyle=z^{*}t_{\bar{g}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}}(z)\,\psi_{\bar{g}}(z^{*}t_{\bar{h}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{h}}(z))

\displaystyle=z^{*}t_{\bar{g}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}}^{*})\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{*}(t_{\bar{g}}\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}}))^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{*}t_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}(z)

\displaystyle=c_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}.

By the same cocycle-untwisting argument as in Theorem
3.9

(equivalently, Fact
3.4

applied to the induced action on
M_{\omega}
), there exists
s\in U(M_{\omega})
such that

c_{\bar{g}}=s^{*}\psi_{\bar{g}}(s),\qquad\forall\bar{g}\in G/K.

Since
M_{\omega}=(M_{\omega})^{K}
, we may choose a representing sequence

s=(s_{n})
with
s_{n}\in U(M^{K})
. Set
w_{n}:=z_{n}s_{n}^{*}\in U(M^{K})
.
Because
s\in M_{\omega}
is central, we still have
\alpha=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(w_{n})
.
Also, from the defining equation for
c_{\bar{g}}
,

\psi_{\bar{g}}(w)=t_{\bar{g}}w
in
M^{\omega}
, i.e.,

\|\psi_{\bar{g}}(w_{n})-t_{\bar{g}}w_{n}\|_{2}\to 0,\qquad\forall\bar{g}\in G/K.

The action
\psi:G/K\to\operatorname{Aut}(M\rtimes K)
restricts to a well-defined induced action
\bar{\psi}:G/K\to\operatorname{Aut}(M^{K})
given by
\bar{\psi}_{\bar{g}}(x)=\psi_{\bar{g}}(x)
for all
x\in M^{K}
.
Since
\psi
is outer,
\bar{\psi}
is also an outer action.
By (2), since
t_{\bar{g}}\in M^{K}
,

t_{\bar{g}\bar{h}}=\psi_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}})t_{\bar{g}}=\bar{\psi}_{\bar{g}}(t_{\bar{h}})t_{\bar{g}},

so
t:G/K\to U(M^{K})
is a 1-cocycle of the outer action
\bar{\psi}:G/K\to\operatorname{Aut}(M^{K})
.
(Warning: this is not the usual order for 1-cocycle, but can be done by taking the adjoint)

By Fact
3.4
,
there exists a unitary
v\in U(M^{K})
such that

t_{\bar{g}}=\bar{\psi}_{\bar{g}}(v^{*})v=\psi_{\bar{g}}(v^{*})v,\quad\forall\bar{g}\in G/K

Substitute this coboundary expression into (3), we have

\|\psi_{\bar{g}}(w_{n})-\psi_{\bar{g}}(v^{*})vw_{n}\|_{2}=\|\psi_{\bar{g}}(vw_{n})-vw_{n}\|_{2}\to 0.

Let
\alpha^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ad}(v)\circ\alpha=\operatorname{Ad}(v)\circ\lim\operatorname{Ad}(w_{n})=\lim\operatorname{Ad}(vw_{n})\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)
.
Since
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha
are equivalent modulo
\operatorname{Int}(M)
, this means
[\alpha^{\prime}]=[\alpha]
.

Since also
vw_{n}\in M^{K}
, the sequence is asymptotically
G
-fixed by (4). Therefore

u_{n}:=E_{M^{G}}(vw_{n})=\frac{1}{|G/K|}\sum_{\bar{g}\in G/K}\psi_{\bar{g}}(vw_{n})\in M^{G}

satisfies

\|u_{n}-vw_{n}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{|G/K|}\|\psi_{\bar{g}}(vw_{n})-vw_{n}\|_{2}\to 0.

After taking polar parts as in Lemma
4.11
, we may assume
u_{n}\in U(M^{G})
and still

\|u_{n}-vw_{n}\|_{2}\to 0.

Because
vw_{n}
implements
\alpha^{\prime}
up to
\omega
-limit, the same is true for
u_{n}
, so
\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
.
Hence the isomorphism class of
Y
determines a class

[\alpha]\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M).

Therefore, we establish the equivalence

\operatorname{Inv}({\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K))_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M).\qed

Theorem 4.14
.

Suppose outer action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is not approximately inner,
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
.
The group of invertible
G/K
-liftable bimodules in the
G/K
-deequivariantization
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G/K}
is equivalent to the group of the intersection of
G
-centrally trivial automorphisms and
G
-approximately inner automorphisms
\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
modulo inner automorphisms, i.e.,

\operatorname{Inv}\left(\bigoplus\limits_{g\in G/K}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M\rtimes K)\boxtimes_{M\rtimes K}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M\rtimes K))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M\rtimes K)\right)_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M).

Proof.

Let
N:=M\rtimes K
.
By Proposition
4.13
,
\operatorname{Inv}({\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N))_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M)
,
every liftable invertible approximately inner
N
-
N
bimodule is of the form
{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)

for some
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
, where
\widetilde{\alpha}(au_{k})=\alpha(a)u_{k}
,
a\in M
,
k\in K
.
Moreover, the isomorphism class of
{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)
depends only on the class of

\alpha
in
\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)/\operatorname{Int}(M)
.

We claim that for
\alpha\in\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M)
and
g\in G/K
,

{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)\in\bigl({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(N)\boxtimes_{N}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(N)\bigr)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\alpha_{\omega}=(\psi_{g})_{\omega}\qquad\text{on }M_{\omega}

Indeed, since
{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(N)
is invertible, the above condition is equivalent to
{}_{\psi_{g}^{-1}\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(N)
.
For an invertible bimodule
{}_{\theta}L^{2}(N)
, being centrally trivial is equivalent to
\theta\in\operatorname{Ct}(N)
, i.e.,
\theta_{\omega}=\operatorname{id}_{N_{\omega}}
.
Hence

{}_{\psi_{g}^{-1}\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(N)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad(\psi_{g}^{-1}\widetilde{\alpha})_{\omega}=\operatorname{id}_{N_{\omega}}

By Corollary
3.11
,

N_{\omega}=(M\rtimes K)_{\omega}\cong(M_{\omega})^{K}=M_{\omega}.

Under this identification,
\widetilde{\alpha}_{\omega}
acts as
\alpha_{\omega}
, since
\widetilde{\alpha}

fixes every
u_{k}
, and the induced action of
g\in G/K
on
N_{\omega}

is exactly
(\psi_{g})_{\omega}
. Therefore

(\psi_{g}^{-1}\widetilde{\alpha})_{\omega}=\operatorname{id}_{N_{\omega}}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad(\psi_{g}^{-1}\alpha)_{\omega}=\operatorname{id}_{M_{\omega}}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\alpha_{\omega}=(\psi_{g})_{\omega}.

By definition, the condition
\alpha_{\omega}=(\psi_{g})_{\omega}
for some
g\in G/K
is exactly
the condition
\alpha\in\mathrm{GCt}(M)
. Hence a liftable invertible approximately inner bimodule

{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)\in\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}\bigl({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(N)\boxtimes_{N}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(N)\bigr)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N)\quad\Longleftarrow\alpha\in\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M).

Thus the correspondence
[{}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}L^{2}(N)]\mapsto[\alpha]

induces a bijection

\operatorname{Inv}\left(\bigoplus_{g\in G/K}\left({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(N)\boxtimes_{N}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(N)\right)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(N)\right)_{\mathsf{lift}}\cong(\mathrm{GCt}(M)\cap\overline{\mathrm{GInt}}(M))/\operatorname{Int}(M).\qed

5.
Example

5.1.
Infinite tensor product of non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factors

Suppose
N_{i}
are non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factors,
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N_{i}
is the countable infinite tensor product of
N_{i}
, which is a McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor.
We denote
M_{n}:=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{n}N_{i}
and
P_{n}=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i>n}N_{i}
for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, then
M=M_{n}\overline{\otimes}P_{n}
and
M=\left(\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}M_{n}\right)^{\prime\prime}
.

Since
M_{n}
is a finite tensor product of non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factors,
M_{n}
is also non-Gamma.
By
[
Pop10
, Prop. 3.2.1]

M_{n}
has spectral gap in
M
, i.e.,
for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in U(M_{n})
and
\delta>0
such that if
a\in M
satisfies
\|[a,u_{i}]\|_{2}\leq\delta\|a\|_{2}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
, then
\|a-E_{M_{n}^{\prime}\cap M}(a)\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}

[
Pop10
, Def. 2.1]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
E_{P_{n}}=\operatorname{tr}_{M_{n}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{P_{n}}:M\to P_{n}
be conditional expectation.
For
(a_{n})\in M^{\omega}
,
(a_{n})
is a central sequence if and only if
\lim\limits_{n\to\omega}\|E_{P_{k}}(a_{n})-a_{n}\|_{2}=0
for each
k
.

Proof.

Since
M
is an inductive limit of subfactors
M_{n}
, which has spectral gap in
M
. Then by
[
Pop10
, Lem. 2.2]
,
M_{\omega}=M^{\prime}\cap M^{\omega}=\bigcap_{n}(M_{n}^{\prime}\cap M)^{\omega}
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Suppose
Q\subseteq P
is a
\rm II_{1}
subfactor. Let
r\geq 1
,
A:=M_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes P
with normalized trace
\operatorname{tr}_{A}:=\operatorname{tr}_{r}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{P}
.
Suppose
p\in M_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes(Q^{\prime}\cap P)
is a nonzero projection and

\alpha:P\to pAp
is a normal unital
*
-homomorphism.
Define
\rho:Q\to pAp
by
\rho(x):=p(1_{r}\otimes x)
.
It is a well-defined unital
*
-homomorphism since
p
commutes with
1_{r}\otimes Q
.

Suppose there is some
0<\delta<1
such that

\|\alpha(v)-\rho(v)\|_{2,p}\leq\delta,\qquad\quad\forall v\in U(Q),

where
\|\cdot\|_{2,p}
is the
L^{2}
-norm on
pAp
given by

\operatorname{tr}_{p}(x):=\operatorname{tr}_{pAp}(x)=\operatorname{tr}_{A}(x)/\operatorname{tr}_{A}(p).

Then there exists a nonzero partial isometry
w\in pAp
such that

\alpha(x)w=w\rho(x)=wp(1_{r}\otimes x),\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q.

Proof.

For each
v\in U(Q)
, let
y_{v}:=\alpha(v)\rho(v)^{*}
.
Since
\alpha(v)\in pAp
and
\rho(v)\in pAp
is unitary, we have
y_{v}\in pAp
and

\|y_{v}-p\|_{2,p}=\|\alpha(v)\rho(v)^{*}-p\|_{2.p}=\|\alpha(v)-\rho(v)\|_{2,p}\leq\delta.

Let

K_{0}:=\{y_{v}\mid v\in U(Q)\}\subseteq pAp,

and
K
be the
\|\cdot\|_{2,p}
-closed convex hull of
K_{0}
in the Hilbert space
L^{2}(pAp,\operatorname{tr}_{p})
.
Every element of
K_{0}
is in the closed ball of radius
\delta
around
p
, so is for every element of
K
:

\|\eta-p\|_{2,p}\leq\delta,\qquad\quad\forall\eta\in K.

Since
K
is a nonempty closed convex subset of a Hilbert space, there exists a unique element
\xi\in K
with minimal
L^{2}
-norm.

For
u\in U(Q)
, define
T_{u}:L^{2}(pAp,\operatorname{tr}_{p})\to L^{2}(pAp,\operatorname{tr}_{p})
by

T_{u}(\eta):=\alpha(u)\eta\rho(u)^{*}.

This is an isometry since
\alpha(u)
and
\rho(u)
are unitaries in
pAp
.
Moreover,
T_{u}(K_{0})=K_{0}
since for
v\in U(Q)
,

T_{u}(y_{v})=\alpha(u)\alpha(v)\rho(v)^{*}\rho(u)^{*}=\alpha(uv)\rho(uv)^{*}=y_{uv},

and hence
T_{u}(K)=K
.
By the uniqueness of the minimal norm element in
K
, we have

T_{u}(\xi)=\xi,\qquad\quad\forall u\in U(Q).

In other words,

\alpha(u)\xi=\xi\rho(u),\qquad\quad\forall u\in U(Q).

By linearity and normality,

\alpha(x)\xi=\xi\rho(x),\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q.

This
\xi
is nonzero, since

\|\xi-p\|_{2,p}\ \leq\delta<1=\|p\|_{2,p}.

Take the polar decomposition of
\xi=w|\xi|
in
pAp
, where
w\in pAp
is a nonzero partial isometry. We shall show
w
also intertwines
\alpha
and
\rho
.
Note that
\alpha(x)\xi=\xi\rho(x)
for all
x\in Q
.
Take adjoints, we have
\xi^{*}\alpha(x^{*})=\rho(x^{*})\xi^{*}
.
Replacing
x
by
x^{*}
, we have

\xi^{*}\alpha(x)=\rho(x)\xi^{*},\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q.

Then

\xi^{*}\xi\rho(x)=\xi^{*}\alpha(x)\xi=\rho(x)\xi^{*}\xi,\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q.

Thus
\xi^{*}\xi\in\rho(Q)^{\prime}\cap pAp
, which implies

|\xi|\in\rho(Q)^{\prime}\cap pAp
.
Then by the uniqueness of polar decomposition, we have

\alpha(x)w=w\rho(x),\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q.\qed

Proposition 5.3
.

A bifinite bimodule
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
if and only if there exist a finite
n
and
Y\in\mathsf{Bim}(M_{n})
such that
X\cong Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1
, it is clear that
Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})
is centrally trivial.

Now suppose
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
.
The category of bifinite bimodule is semisimple. Suppose
X\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}
,
X_{i}
are indecomposable
M
-
M
bimodules.
We know that
X_{i}\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
, since central triviality passes to direct summand.
Suppose we know that
X_{i}\cong Y_{i}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n_{i}})
,
Y_{i}\in\mathsf{Bim}(M_{n_{i}})
for all
i=1,\dots,t
.
Let
m=\max_{i=1,\dots,t}\{n_{i}\}
.
Since

M=M_{n_{i}}\overline{\otimes}\left(\overline{\otimes}_{i=n_{j}+1}^{m}N_{i}\right)\overline{\otimes}P_{m}

we have
L^{2}(P_{n_{i}})\cong L^{2}(\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=n_{j}+1}^{m}N_{i})\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{m})
, which follows that

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}X_{i}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}Y_{i}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n_{i}})\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}(Y_{i}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(\overline{\otimes}_{i=n_{j}+1}^{m}N_{i}))\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{m})

\displaystyle\cong\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{t}(Y_{i}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(\overline{\otimes}_{i=n_{j}+1}^{m}N_{i}))\right)\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{m}).

Therefore, it suffices to prove the proposition for indecomposable bimodules.

Since
X
is bifinite,
X\cong{}_{\alpha}[p({\mathbb{C}}^{r}\otimes L^{2}(M))]
for some projection
p\in M_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M
and a normal unital
*
-homomorphism
\alpha:M\to p(M_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M)p
.
This
p
is not necessarily to commute with any
P_{n}
.
To fix this, since
M
is inductive limit of
M_{n}
, one can find a sufficiently large
n
and a projection
q\in M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M_{n}\subseteq M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M
with

(\operatorname{tr}_{s}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{M})(q)=(\operatorname{tr}_{r}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{M})(p).

Then
p
and
q
are Murray-von Neumann equivalent in a matrix amplification of
M
.
This equivalence yields to a unitary isomorphisms of the right
M
-modules between
p({\mathbb{C}}^{r}\otimes L^{2}(M))
and
q({\mathbb{C}}^{s}\otimes L^{2}(M))
.
Conjugating the left action by this right module isomorphism, we can replace the realization
(p,\alpha)
by
(q,\alpha^{\prime})
.
Thus,

X\cong{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}[q({\mathbb{C}}^{s}\otimes L^{2}(M))].

Since
M_{n}=P_{n}^{\prime}\cap M
, we have

q\in M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M_{n}=M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes(P_{n}^{\prime}\cap M).

Since
X
is centrally trivial, for every central sequence
(a_{k})\in M_{\omega}
,

\|a_{k}\rhd\xi-\xi\lhd a_{k}\|_{2}\to 0,\qquad\quad\forall\xi\in X

In the
{}_{\alpha^{\prime}}[q({\mathbb{C}}^{s}\otimes L^{2}(M))]
model, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\alpha^{\prime}(a_{k})-q(1_{s}\otimes a_{k})\|_{2,q}\to 0,

(1)

where
\|\cdot\|_{2,q}
is the
L^{2}
-norm on
qAq
and
A=M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M
.

By Lemma
5.1
, a bounded sequence is central if and only if it is asymptotically contained in every
P_{m}
.
In particular, any sequence
(v_{k})
is central if
v_{k}\in U(P_{k})
for each
k
.
Therefore, for a fixed small
\delta>0
, there exists
n
such that

\|\alpha^{\prime}(v)-q(1_{s}\otimes v)\|_{2,q}\leq\delta,\qquad\quad\forall v\in U(P_{n}).

Otherwise, for every
k
, one could choose
v_{k}\in U(P_{k})
with

\|\alpha^{\prime}(v_{k})-q(1_{s}\otimes v_{k})\|_{2,q}>\delta,

but
(v_{k})
is central, which contradicts to (
1
).

Now we are able to apply Lemma
5.2
.
Let
Q=P_{n}
,
P=M
and
q\in M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes(Q^{\prime}\cap P)
.
Because
q
commutes with
1_{s}\otimes P_{n}
, we have the standard homomorphism

\rho_{n}:P_{n}\to q(M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M)q,\qquad\rho_{n}(x)=q(1_{s}\otimes x)

Since
X
is centrally trivial, for a fixed small
\delta>0
, there exists
n
such that

\|\alpha^{\prime}(v)-\rho_{n}(v)\|_{2,q}\leq\delta,\qquad\quad\forall v\in U(P_{n}).

According to Lemma
5.2
, there exists a nonzero partial isometry
w\in qAq=q(M_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes M)q
such that

\alpha^{\prime}(x)w=wq(1_{s}\otimes x),\qquad\quad\forall x\in Q=P_{n}

Then we have

\alpha^{\prime}(x)w=w\rho_{n}(x),\qquad\quad\forall x\in P_{n}

We define the algebraic subspace of
P_{n}
-central vectors in
X
:

X_{0}=\{\xi\in X\mid x\rhd\xi=\xi\lhd x,\ \forall x\in P_{n}\}.

There are several properties for
X_{0}
:

•

X_{0}
is non-zero: Consider above nonzero partial isometry
w
, for any
x\in P_{n}
,

x\rhd w=\alpha^{\prime}(x)w=w\rho_{n}(x)=wq(1_{s}\otimes x)=w\lhd x,

so
w\in X_{0}
.

•

X_{0}\in\mathsf{Bim}(M_{n})
: Since
M_{n}
strictly commutes with
P_{n}
in
M
, the left and right
M_{n}
-actions preserve the centralizer condition.

•

X_{0}
has a
M_{n}
-valued inner product:
For any
\xi,\eta\in X_{0}
and
x\in P_{n}
, the usual
M
-valued inner from
X
satisfies

x\langle\xi\mid\eta\rangle=\langle\xi\lhd x^{*}\mid\eta\rangle=\langle x^{*}\rhd\xi\mid\eta\rangle=\langle\xi\mid x\rhd\eta\rangle=\langle\xi\mid\eta\lhd x\rangle=\langle\xi\mid\eta\rangle x.

Thus
\langle\xi\mid\eta\rangle\in P_{n}^{\prime}\cap M=M_{n}
.

We define a map from the algebraic tensor product
X_{0}\odot P_{n}
into
X
by:

\Phi(\xi\otimes y)=\xi\lhd y.

This map is an isometry because
for
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in X
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in P_{n}
,

\langle\Phi(\xi_{1}\otimes y_{1}),\Phi(\xi_{2}\otimes y_{2})\rangle_{M}=\langle\xi_{1}\lhd y_{1},\xi_{2}\lhd y_{2}\rangle_{M}=y_{1}^{*}\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{M}y_{2}=\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{M_{n}}\otimes y_{1}^{*}y_{2}

since
\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{M_{n}}\in M_{n}
commutes with
y_{1}^{*}\in P_{n}
.
This
\Phi
extends uniquely to an isometry
X_{0}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})\to X
.

Now we show that
\Phi
is an
M
-
M
bimodule intertwiner.
Let
a\in M_{n}
and
b\in P_{n}
.

•

Right action:
In the spatial tensor product, the right action of
ab
is given by
(\xi\otimes y)\lhd(ab)=(\xi\lhd a)\otimes(yb)
.
Applying
\Phi
to this yields:

\Phi((\xi\lhd a)\otimes(yb))=(\xi\lhd a)\lhd(yb)=\xi\lhd(ayb),

while applying the right action of
ab
directly to the image in
X
:

\Phi(\xi\otimes y)\lhd(ab)=(\xi\lhd y)\lhd(ab)=\xi\lhd(yab).

Since
a\in M_{n}
and
y\in P_{n}
commute,
ayb=yab
. The right actions match.

•

Left action:
In the spatial tensor product, the left action of
ab
is given by
(ab)\rhd(\xi\otimes y)=(a\rhd\xi)\otimes(by)
.
Applying
\Phi
yields:

\Phi((a\rhd\xi)\otimes(by))=(a\rhd\xi)\lhd(by)

Now, apply the left action of
ab
directly to the image in
X
:

(ab)\rhd\Phi(\xi\otimes y)=a\rhd(b\rhd(\xi\lhd y))

Because
X
is a true bimodule, left and right actions commute, so
b\rhd(\xi\lhd y)=(b\rhd\xi)\lhd y
.
Because
\xi\in X_{0}
, the left action of
b\in P_{n}
equals its right action:
b\rhd\xi=\xi\lhd b
.
Substituting this back in gives:

a\rhd((\xi\lhd b)\lhd y)=a\rhd(\xi\lhd(by))=(a\rhd\xi)\lhd(by).

Thus, the left actions also match.

Since
\Phi
is an isometric
M
-
M
bimodule intertwiner, the image of
X_{0}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})
is an
M
-
M
sub-bimodule of
X
.
Because
X
is assumed to be indecomposable, and
X_{0}
is non-empty, we have

X\cong X_{0}\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n}).

Since
X
is bifinite,
X_{0}
is also a bifinite bimodule over
M_{n}
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose
P,Q
are
\rm II_{1}
factors and
P
is non-Gamma.
Suppose
Y\in\mathsf{Bim}(P)
is an indecomposable bifinite bimodule. If
Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q)
and its dual are approximately inner in
\mathsf{Bim}(P\overline{\otimes}Q)
, then
Y\cong L^{2}(P)
.

Proof.

Suppose
X:=Y\oplus L^{2}(P)
.
Let
M:=|X\boxtimes_{P}\overline{X}|
,
P\subseteq M
is a finite index
\rm II_{1}
subfactor. Since
P
is non-Gamma,
M
is also non-Gamma.
Since
X\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q)\cong(Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q))\oplus L^{2}(P\overline{\otimes}Q)
and its dual are approximately inner in
\mathsf{Bim}(P\overline{\otimes}Q)
, so is the tensor product
(X\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q))\boxtimes_{P\overline{\otimes}Q}(\overline{X\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q)})\cong(X\boxtimes_{P}\overline{X})\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q)
, then by
[
CJP24
, Prop. 4.8]
,
P\overline{\otimes}Q\subseteq|(X\boxtimes_{P}\overline{X})\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(Q)|\cong M\overline{\otimes}Q
is an approximately inner subfactor.

By
[
Pop94a
, Def. 1.4 & Prop. 1.2]
, the inclusion
(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}\subseteq(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}
is
[M:P]^{-1}
-Markov, and has finite Pimsner-Popa basis of
(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}\subseteq(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}
.

Since
P
is non-Gamma, and
P\subseteq M
has finite index, by
[
Pop10
, Prop. 3.2.1]
,
P\overline{\otimes}1\subseteq M\overline{\otimes}Q
has spectral gap, then

(P\overline{\otimes}1)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}=((P\overline{\otimes}1)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q))^{\omega}=((P^{\prime}\cap M)\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}.

Furthermore, since
P\overline{\otimes}Q=(P\overline{\otimes}1)\vee(1\overline{\otimes}Q)
, we have

\displaystyle(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}

\displaystyle=((P\overline{\otimes}1)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega})\cap((1\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega})

\displaystyle=((P^{\prime}\cap M)\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}\cap((1\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega})

\displaystyle=(1\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\prime}\cap((P^{\prime}\cap M)\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega},

since
((P^{\prime}\cap M)\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}\subseteq(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}
.
Thus, there is a finite Pimsner-Popa basis
\{m_{i}\}
for
(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}\subseteq(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}
with
m_{i}=\{b_{i}^{(n)}\}
, where each
b_{i}^{(n)}\in(P^{\prime}\cap M)\overline{\otimes}Q
.

Then for each
x\in M
,
x\otimes 1\in M\overline{\otimes}Q\subseteq(M\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}
, since
m_{i}=\{b_{i}^{(n)}\}
is a Pimsner-Popa basis, we have

x\otimes 1=\sum_{i}m_{i}E_{(P\overline{\otimes}Q)^{\omega}}(m_{i}^{*}(x\otimes 1)),

In other words,

\left\|x\otimes 1-\sum_{i}b_{i}^{(n)}E_{P\overline{\otimes}Q}\left((b_{i}^{(n)})^{*}(x\overline{\otimes}1)\right)\right\|_{2}\to 0

Note that
E_{P\overline{\otimes}Q}=E_{P}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{Q}
, because the inclusion
P\overline{\otimes}Q\subseteq M\overline{\otimes}Q
is given by
(P\subseteq M)\overline{\otimes}Q
.
Apply the contractive map
\operatorname{id}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{Q}:L^{2}(M\overline{\otimes}Q)\to L^{2}(M)
, we have

\left\|x-\sum_{i}(\operatorname{id}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{Q})\left(b_{i}^{(n)}(E_{P}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{Q})\left((b_{i}^{(n)})^{*}(x\overline{\otimes}1)\right)\right)\right\|_{2}\to 0.

Since
P\subseteq M
is of finite index,
P^{\prime}\cap M
is finite-dimensional. Choose a basis
v_{1},\dots,v_{r}
of
P^{\prime}\cap M
, we can write

b_{i}^{(n)}=\sum_{j=1}^{r}v_{j}\otimes q_{j},

for some
q_{j}\in Q
.
Hence
(b_{i}^{(n)})^{*}(x\overline{\otimes}1)=\sum_{j=1}^{r}v_{r}^{*}x\otimes q_{j}^{*}
and

b_{i}^{(n)}(E_{P}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{Q})\left((b_{i}^{(n)})^{*}(x\overline{\otimes}1)\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{r}v_{k}\otimes q_{k}\sum_{j=1}^{r}E_{P}(v_{j}^{*}x)\otimes q_{j}^{*}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{r}v_{k}E_{P}(v_{j}^{*}x)\otimes q_{k}q_{j}^{*}

Applying
\operatorname{id}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{Q}
, we obtain

(\operatorname{id}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{Q})\left(b_{i}^{(n)}(E_{P}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{Q})\left((b_{i}^{(n)})^{*}(x\overline{\otimes}1)\right)\right)=\sum_{j,k=1}^{r}\operatorname{tr}_{Q}(q_{k}q_{j}^{*})v_{k}E_{P}(v_{j}^{*}x)

Each
v_{k}\in P^{\prime}\cap M
and each
E_{P}(v_{j}^{*}x)\in P
, so this belongs to
P\vee(P^{\prime}\cap M)
.
Therefore,
x
is in the
L^{2}
-closure of
P\vee(P^{\prime}\cap M)
, which is
P\vee(P^{\prime}\cap M)
.
This proves that
M=P\vee(P^{\prime}\cap M)
.

According to
[
CJP24
, Prop. 4.14]
, this implies that
L^{2}(M)\cong\bigoplus_{i}L^{2}(P)
as
P
-
P
bimodules.
On the other hand,
L^{2}(M)\cong X\boxtimes_{P}\overline{X}\cong L^{2}(P)\oplus Y\oplus\overline{Y}\oplus(Y\boxtimes_{P}\overline{Y})
.
Since
Y
is indecomposable and appeared in the direct summand of
L^{2}(M)
,
Y\cong L^{2}(P)
.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
.

Proof.

Suppose
X\in\tilde{\chi}(M)
is indecomposable.
Since
X
is centrally trivial, by Proposition
5.3
,
X\cong Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})
, where
Y\cong\mathsf{Bim}(M_{n})
. Since
X
is indecomposable,
Y
is also indecomposable.
By the definition of
\tilde{\chi}(M)
, it is the dualizable part of
{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M)\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
, so
X
and its dual are both approximately inner.
Note that
M_{n}
is non-Gamma, by Lemma
5.4
,
Y\cong L^{2}(M_{n})
.
Therefore,
X\cong L^{2}(M_{n})\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})\cong L^{2}(M)
, and hence
\tilde{\chi}(M)\cong\mathsf{Hilb}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

Recall a
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
is s-McDuff if
M\cong N\overline{\otimes}R
for some non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor
N
.
Connes asked when
M
is McDuff, whether it is true that
M
is s-McDuff if and only if
\chi(M)
is trivial.
Popa gives a negative answer to Connes’ question by constructing the example of countable infinite spatial tensor product of non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factors
M=\overline{\otimes}_{i}N_{i}
which is McDuff but not s-McDuff
[
Pop10
]
.

In
[
CJP24
, Ex. 4.16]
, authors prove that the categorical
\tilde{\chi}
of s-McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor is always trivial, so they asked whether Connes’ conjecture holds for the categorical
\tilde{\chi}(M)
.
Here we show that
M
from Popa’s construction also has trivial
\tilde{\chi}(M)
.
Therefore, this also gives an negative answer their question.

5.2.
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
for entry-wise action

Fact 5.7
.

Let
M
be a separable finite factor and
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(M)
.
In the injective case, Connes showed that approximate innerness can be characterized by the presence of almost central vectors in the associated bimodule
[
Con76
, Thm. 3.1]
.
Mingo later removes the injectivity assumption: he proves that, for arbitrary separable finite von Neumann algebras, an automorphism is approximately inner if and only if it is approximately inner as a completely positive map, and that this is equivalent to weak containment of the identity correspondence in the correspondence associated with
\alpha

[
Min90
]
.
Therefore,

\alpha\not\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad L^{2}(M)\npreceq{}_{\alpha}L^{2}(M).

Since weak containment of
L^{2}(M)
in a correspondence is equivalent to the existence of almost central unit vectors, its failure is equivalent, by a standard contrapositive argument, to the existence of a finite set
F\subseteq M
and a constant
C>0
such that

\|x\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sum_{a\in F}\|\beta(a)x-xa\|_{2}^{2}.\qquad\forall x\in L^{2}(M).

Proposition 5.8
.

Suppose
P\overline{\otimes}Q
is tensor product of two
\rm II_{1}
factors.
Suppose
X\in\mathsf{Bim}(P)
and
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(Q)
. Then
X\overline{\otimes}{}_{\alpha}L^{2}(Q)\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(P\overline{\otimes}Q)
implies
\alpha\in{\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(Q)
.

Proof.

We prove the contrapositive.
Denote
H={}_{\alpha}L^{2}(Q)
.
For each
a\in F
, define the bounded linear map
T_{a}:H\to H
by

T_{a}(\xi)=a\rhd\xi-\xi\lhd a=\alpha(a)\xi-\xi a.

Suppose
\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(Q)
is not approximately inner, by Fact
5.7
, then there exist
C>0
and finite subset
F\subseteq Q
such that

\|\xi\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sum_{a\in F}\|\alpha(a)\xi-\xi a\|_{2}^{2}=C\sum_{a\in F}\|T_{a}(\xi)\|_{2}^{2}

for all
\xi\in H
.
In terms of
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued inner products, this means

\langle\xi\mid\xi\rangle\leq C\sum_{a\in F}\langle T_{a}\xi\mid T_{a}\xi\rangle=\left\langle\xi\,\middle|\,C\sum_{a\in F}T_{a}^{*}T_{a}\xi\right\rangle,

which is equivalent to say

\operatorname{id}_{H}\leq C\sum_{a\in F}T_{a}^{*}T_{a}

as positive operators on
H
.

Now tensor this inequality with
\operatorname{id}_{X}
. On the Hilbert space
X\overline{\otimes}H
, we have

\operatorname{id}_{X}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{H}\leq\sum_{a\in F}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\otimes T_{a})^{*}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\otimes T_{a}),

therefore for every
\eta\in X\overline{\otimes}H
,

\|\eta\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sum_{a\in F}\|(\operatorname{id}_{X}\otimes T_{a})\eta\|_{2}^{2}=C\sum_{a\in F}\|(1\otimes\alpha(a))\eta-\eta(1\otimes a)\|_{2}^{2}.

Suppose
X\overline{\otimes}H\in\mathsf{Bim}(P\overline{\otimes}Q)
is approximately inner, then there exists an approximately inner
(X\overline{\otimes}H)_{P\overline{\otimes}Q}
basis
\{y_{i}^{(n)}\}
.
For all
1\otimes a\in P\overline{\otimes}Q
,

\|(1\otimes a)\rhd y_{i}^{(n)}-y_{i}^{(n)}\lhd(1\otimes a)\|_{2}\to 0.

Then we have

\|y_{i}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sum_{a\in F}\|(1\otimes\alpha(a))\eta-\eta(1\otimes a)\|_{2}^{2}\to 0.

This implies that

\sum_{i}|y_{i}^{(n)}\rangle\langle y_{i}^{(n)}|\to 0\qquad\mathrm{strongly},

since
\{y_{i}^{(n)}\}
is uniformly bounded in the module norm.
However, as an approximate basis,

\sum_{i}|y_{i}^{(n)}\rangle\langle y_{i}^{(n)}|\to\operatorname{id}_{X}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{H}\qquad\mathrm{strongly},

which is a contradiction.
∎

Construction 5.9
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group.
Popa and Shlyakhtenko
showed that every subfactor standard invariant can be realized as an
LF_{\infty}
subfactor
[
PS03
]
.
Guionnet-Jones-Shlyakhtenko provide an alternative
realization of finite depth standard invariants as inclusions of interpolated free group factors
[
GJS10
]
.
In other words, there exists a non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor
N
and an outer action
\alpha:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(N)
.

Let
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N
to be the countable infinite spatial tensor product of
N
.
We define
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
by
\psi_{g}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{g}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

The group action
\psi
in Construction
5.9
is not centrally trivial.

Proof.

Fix
g\neq e
. Since

\alpha_{g}
is outer, we choose
a\in N
such that
\|\alpha_{g}(a)-a\|_{2}=\epsilon>0

For each
n
, let

a_{n}=1\otimes\cdots\otimes 1\otimes a\otimes 1\otimes\cdots\in P_{n-1}\subseteq M

with
a
at the
n
-th tensor.
Then
(a_{n})
is a central sequence by Lemma
5.1
.
Note that

\psi_{g}(a_{n})=1\otimes\cdots\otimes 1\otimes\alpha_{g}(a)\otimes 1\otimes\cdots,

so

\|\psi_{g}(a_{n})-a_{n}\|_{2}=\|\alpha_{g}(a)-a\|_{2}=\epsilon\neq 0.

Therefore,
\psi
is not centrally trivial.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

The group action
\psi
in Construction
5.9
is not approximately inner.

Proof.

We know that
M=N\overline{\otimes}P_{1}
and
\psi_{g}=\alpha_{g}\otimes\beta_{g}
, where
\beta_{g}\in\operatorname{Aut}(P_{1})
.
Suppose
\psi_{g}
is approximately inner, then
{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)={}_{\alpha_{g}}L^{2}(N)\otimes{}_{\beta_{g}}L^{2}(P_{1})
is approximately inner.
By Proposition
5.8
, this implies
\alpha_{g}\in\operatorname{Aut}(N)
is approximately inner. However, since
N
is non-Gamma so that every approximately inner automorphism must be inner, which contradicts to the construction that
\alpha_{g}
is outer when
g\neq e
.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Suppose
X\in{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M)
,
{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)\boxtimes_{M}X
is not approximately inner when
g\neq e
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.3
, any centrally trivial bimodule
X\cong Y\overline{\otimes}L^{2}(P_{n})
for some
Y\in\mathsf{Bim}(M_{n})
.
Note that
\psi_{g}=\sigma_{g}\otimes\theta_{g}
, where
\sigma_{g}=\overline{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{g}\in\operatorname{Aut}(M_{n})
and
\theta_{g}=\overline{\otimes}_{i>n}\alpha_{g}\in\operatorname{Aut}(P_{n})
, then

{}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)\boxtimes_{M}X\cong({}_{\sigma_{g}}L^{2}(M_{n})\boxtimes_{M_{n}}Y)\overline{\otimes}({}_{\theta_{g}}L^{2}(P_{n}))

Suppose this is approximately inner, by Proposition
5.8
, it implies that
\theta_{g}\in\operatorname{Aut}(P_{n})
is approximately inner.
However,
\theta_{g}
on
P_{n}
is given by the same way as
\psi_{g}
on
M
, by Proposition
5.11
,
\theta_{g}
is not approximately inner for all
g\neq e
, which is a contradiction.
∎

For the given action
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
, according to Proposition
5.10
and
5.11
,
K=\psi^{-1}(\operatorname{Ct}(M))\cap G
and
L=\psi^{-1}({\overline{\operatorname{Int}}}(M))\cap G
are trivial.
By Theorem
4.8
,
we have
G
-equivariantization of
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)
,

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G}\cong\bigoplus_{g\in G}({}_{\psi_{g}}L^{2}(M)\boxtimes_{M}{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ct}}(M))\cap{\mathsf{Bim}}_{\operatorname{ai}}(M)

as unitary
G
-crossed braided tensor categories.
By Proposition
5.5
, we know the
g=e
component is equivalent to
\mathsf{Hilb}
; and by Proposition
5.12
, the
g\neq e
component is empty.
Therefore,

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G}\cong\mathsf{Hilb}

as unitary
G
-crossed braided tensor categories.

Applying
G
-equivariantization on
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G}
, we have

\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)\cong(\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes G)_{G})^{G}\cong\mathsf{Hilb}^{G}\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G).

We can summarize above results into the following theorem.

Theorem 5.13
.

Suppose
G
is a finite group,
N
is a non-Gamma
\rm II_{1}
factor and
\alpha:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(N)
is an outer action. Let
M=\overline{\bigotimes}_{i=1}^{\infty}N
be the countable infinite spatial tensor product of
N
and
\psi:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)
is given by
\psi_{g}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{g}
.
Then
\tilde{\chi}(M\rtimes_{\psi}G)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G)
as unitary braided tensor categories.

Corollary 5.14
.

For any finite group
G
, its unitary representation category
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
can be realized as
\tilde{\chi}(M)
for some McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factor
M
.

Note that
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
is a symmetric braided category, which is not modular.
In particular, it is not the Drinfeld center of a unitary fusion category.

Corollary 5.15
.

There exist McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factors
M
whose
\tilde{\chi}(M)
is not modular.

Fact 5.16
.

Let
A_{n}
be the alternating group on
n
letters.
The groups of characters
\widehat{A_{5}}\cong\widehat{A_{6}}
are trivial while the categories of unitary representations
\mathsf{Rep}(A_{5})\ncong\mathsf{Rep}(A_{6})
.

Proposition 5.17
.

For any finite abelian group
A
, there exist finite groups
G
and
H
such that
\widehat{G}\cong\widehat{H}\cong A
but
\mathsf{Rep}(G)\ncong\mathsf{Rep}(H)
.

Proof.

Let
A
be an arbitrary finite abelian group. Let
G:=A\times A_{5}
and
H:=A\times A_{6}
.
Note that
\operatorname{Hom}(-\to U(1))
preserves finite direct product, we have

\widehat{G}=\widehat{A\times A_{5}}\cong\widehat{A}\times\widehat{A_{5}}\cong A\times\{e\}\cong A\cong\widehat{H}.

It is known that for finite groups
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
,

\mathsf{Rep}(G_{1}\times G_{2})\cong\mathsf{Rep}(G_{1})\boxtimes\mathsf{Rep}(G_{2}),

as unitary braided tensor categories, where
\boxtimes
is the Deligne tensor product.
Combine this with Fact
5.16
,

\mathsf{Rep}(G)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(A_{5})\boxtimes\mathsf{Rep}(A)\ncong\mathsf{Rep}(A_{6})\boxtimes\mathsf{Rep}(A)\cong\mathsf{Rep}(H).\qed

Corollary 5.18
.

For any finite abelian group
A
,
there exist McDuff
\rm II_{1}
factors
M
and
N
such that
\chi(M)\cong\chi(N)\cong A
, but
\tilde{\chi}(M)\ncong\tilde{\chi}(N)
.
